73	## Pull Freshness
74	
75	Preflight auto-pulls the agent's repo so boot sees the team's latest pushed state, and stamps the time of each successful pull inside the repo's git directory. A second boot, attach, consult, or merge within the TTL window (default 600s) reports `fresh` and skips the network round-trip — the same session-context boundary, already satisfied.
76	
77	Pass `--fresh` to bypass the cache (what `/lr:pull-lore` does), `--ttl <seconds>` to change the window, or `--no-pull` to skip the pull entirely. The full pull semantics — `--ff-only`, fail-fast transport env vars, the skip and failure cases — are specified in `pull_repo()`'s own comments in `scripts/lr-core`; `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` is a short pointer into it, not a second copy.
78

85	pull+version-compare or skip, teammate detection), then the docstrings of the four functions it
86	names for the exact hand commands — `_resolve_agent` (agent discovery), `pull_repo` (the
87	`git pull --ff-only` invocation, fail-fast env vars, and the `.git/lr-last-pull` TTL file),
88	`compare_versions` (the match / skew / unknown rules), and `detect_teammate` (the
89	`ps -o args= -p <ppid>` walk and the `--agent-id` match rule).
90	
91	Execute each step by hand to produce the same values preflight would have reported, then **rejoin

93	is the only place that says what to *do* with a version verdict (read `version-check.md` on a

96	the manual path to reach the same end state the script would have produced — not merely to collect

108	
109	## Your Lore
110	
111	Your lore is stored in the `lore/` directory within your agent directory. It is a collection of plain markdown files — each file is a **lore topic**, an atomic piece of knowledge.
112	
113	Lore topics contain:
114	- Decisions and their reasoning
115	- Domain knowledge and discoveries
116	- Operational recommendations from experience
117	- Practical context about the systems and environment you work in
118	
119	Lore topics reference each other by filename, forming a knowledge graph. Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.
120	
121	### Searching Your Lore
122	
123	`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Never act on assumptions about things you might have encountered in previous sessions without first confirming in your lore.
124	
125	Default lore search means `lore/` only. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Search or read `archive/` only when a human explicitly asks about full session logs / a particular session, or when you have a concrete reason that session-log evidence is important for the task.
126	
127	When you need to search or recall lore, read `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` and follow the procedure there.
128	
129	## Your Workdir
130	
131	Your agent directory contains a `workdir/` directory. This is your persistent workspace for files, scripts, tools, and any other artifacts you create or need across sessions.
132	
133	You decide the internal structure of `workdir/` — organize it however makes sense for your work.
134	
135	## Workspace Visibility
136	
137	You have access to the entire workspace — all sibling repositories, data, and resources. Your lore is specific to you, but your reach is workspace-wide.
138	
139	## Collaborating with Other Agents
140	
141	The user may invoke any of three cross-agent mechanisms during the session:
142	
143	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore across the **currently loaded** agents (you, plus any attached guests). Fans out to one subagent per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` and `lore-search.md`.
144	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — ask an **unloaded** agent a focused question. A subagent boots the consultant, answers, and exits. You get back a synthesis plus pointers to specific lore topics or workdir tools you can read or use directly. No finalization for the consultant. See `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md`.
145	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a **guest** into this session for sustained co-work. You remain the sole executor (host); the guest's role and lore-context join yours. Subsequent recalls fan out to the guest too, and finalization iterates per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/attach.md`.
146	
147	Rough rule: recall is for lore you already have loaded; consult is a one-shot question with file handover; attach is for sustained multi-turn work spanning multiple agents' knowledge.
148	
149	## Session Finalization
150	
151	At the end of a session, when the user triggers finalization, you preserve what you learned. This is a two-step process:
152	
153	1. **Reflection** — extract what's worth keeping into reflection topics. Triggered by `/lr:reflect`.
154	2. **Merge** — a separate step integrates reflections into your lore. Triggered by `/lr:merge`.
155	
156	Both steps together: `/lr:finalize`.
157	
158	If guests are attached to this session (via `/lr:attach`), both reflection and merge iterate per active agent in host-first order — each agent learns what fits its role. See `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` for the iteration mechanics.
159	
160	Do not perform finalization unless the user explicitly triggers it.
161

9

12	| **framework-root** | Self-locate (Step 0). `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` also expands to it and may be used as a literal-path fallback. |

14	| **subagent-spawn** | The `Agent` tool. Fan-out = **N parallel `Agent` calls in a single message**. Sub-agent types: `general-purpose` (write), `Explore` (read-only, but excerpt-based — it locates material rather than reviewing it in depth), and `fork` (**inherits the caller's full conversation context**). Each subagent boots as its target agent and reads the procedure doc itself. Two traps: (1) `fork` is unsuitable wherever a subagent must be *independent* of the caller's reasoning — it carries the caller's context by design; (2) passing a **`name`** makes the call an Agent-Teams teammate, and **a teammate does not auto-return its final report to the caller** — only an unnamed call does. Spawn unnamed when you need the result back, or instruct a named teammate to `SendMessage` its report before going idle. |

1	# Version Check
2	
3	Invoked from `agent-boot.md` when the agent's repo `version` differs from the framework `VERSION`. Reconciles the booting agent's repo to the current framework version automatically.
4	
5	## Inputs
6	
7	- `R` — `version` field from the booting agent's repo `lore-repo.md`
8	- `F` — contents of `<framework-root>/VERSION` (trimmed)
9	
10	## Cases
11	
12	### `R == F`
13	
14	You should not be reading this doc — `agent-boot.md`'s version check skipped this file. Treat as a no-op and continue boot.
15	
16	### `R > F`
17	
18	The repo is stamped at a version newer than the installed framework. The plugin may be out of date.
19	
20	- Print a warning to the user. Use the active engine profile to give the engine-specific next step:
21	  - **Codex:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your Codex plugin is older than the repo. Refresh the plugin (`codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`; if the marketplace is Git-backed, run `codex plugin marketplace upgrade lore-framework` first), restart Codex, then boot again. See <framework-root>/INSTALL-CODEX.md.`
22	  - **Cursor:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your Cursor plugin/session is older than the repo. Refresh the plugin source per your install method, start a fresh Cursor session, then boot again. See <framework-root>/INSTALL-CURSOR.md.`
23	  - **Other engines:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your plugin may be out of date. Refresh or reinstall the plugin using your engine's normal install flow, then boot again.`
24	- Do NOT modify any files.
25	- Continue boot in degraded mode.
26	
27	### `R < F`
28	
29	The repo is behind. Run the upgrade procedure below.
30	
31	### Neither — `R` and `F` are not both numeric
32	
33	`lr-core preflight` reports this as `data.version.verdict == "differs"`: the two stamps are
34	unequal, but at least one is not an integer, so there is no ordering between them and none of the
35	cases above applies. **Do not guess a direction** — picking `R > F` or `R < F` here is a coin flip
36	that either skips a needed migration or tells the user to reinstall a perfectly current plugin.
37	
38	- Print one line: `<lore-agent-repo>: version stamp R does not compare with framework version F — reconcile manually.`
39	- Do NOT modify any files and do NOT run the upgrade procedure.
40	- Continue boot in degraded mode.
41	
42	## Upgrade Procedure
43	
44	### Step 1: Pre-flight collision check
45	
46	A blanket "any uncommitted change blocks the upgrade" rule was the historical gate, but it overfired in normal use: lore agent repos routinely carry uncommitted runtime state (other agents' `workdir/*` files — pulse logs, watch markers, scratch artifacts) that cannot collide with what the upgrade writes. The gate is now scoped to **actual collisions** — files git would refuse to overwrite cleanly when the upgrade applies its writes — and to **structural inconsistencies** (conflict markers).
47	
48	**First, confirm the repo is its own git root — with this exact comparison, not an equivalent-seeming one.** Run both of the following and compare their output as plain strings:
49	
50	```
51	git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" rev-parse --show-toplevel
52	python3 -c "import os,sys; print(os.path.realpath(sys.argv[1]))" "<lore-agent-repo>"
53	```
54	
55	**Use `python3 -c` for the second command, not `cd "<lore-agent-repo>" && pwd`.** They look equivalent and usually agree, but on macOS `pwd` (without `-P`) returns the *logical* path — it does not resolve symlinks — while git's `--show-toplevel` always returns the *physical*, symlink-resolved one. `/var` is itself a symlink to `/private/var` on macOS, so a repo under a `/var/...` tmpdir (a `TMPDIR`-based fixture, for instance) makes the two commands disagree even when the repo genuinely is its own git root — a false mismatch that skips a real, needed upgrade. `os.path.realpath()` resolves symlinks the same way `git_toplevel()` does in `scripts/lr-core`, so it's the one method guaranteed to agree with the script's own verdict; treat it as the specified command, not one option among several.
56	
57	If the two outputs differ, the lore repo is *not* its own repository — it sits inside an enclosing one (the workspace-meta-repo layout is supported), and `git -C` silently retargets every command at that enclosing repo. **Do not run the collision check in that case**: the status output would be rooted at the wrong repo, so Step 1c's write-set intersection would compare repo-relative globs against wrong-rooted paths, find a false-empty intersection, and let Step 2 overwrite the user's uncommitted edits. Instead print `<lore-agent-repo>: not its own git root — skipping automatic upgrade, reconcile manually.`, do not modify any files, and continue boot in degraded mode. (This is the same guard `git_toplevel()` applies in `scripts/lr-core`; the rule is stated there in full.)
58	
59	Then run `git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" status --porcelain` (quote the substituted path so it survives spaces; use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing — the shell CWD is shared with Glob, Grep, and subsequent git calls, so a stray `cd` silently shifts their root for the rest of the session). Untracked files (`??`) are ignored throughout — the upgrade never overwrites a file git doesn't already track.
60	
61	#### 1a. Conflict markers — always defer
62	
63	`git status --porcelain` exposes two distinct signals of an in-progress merge:
64	
65	- **Unmerged status codes:** `UU`, `AA`, `DD`, `AU`, `UA`, `UD`, `DU` — git itself is reporting the file as actively conflicted. Treat any such code as a hard-defer condition without further inspection.
66	- **Modified files containing conflict markers:** for files marked with the modified codes (`M `, ` M`, `MM`, `AM`, `MD`, `RM`, `CM`, etc. — anything where the working-tree side is `M`), scan for unresolved markers.
67	
68	The marker scan must use **precise anchors** to avoid false-positives on legitimate Markdown:
69	
70	- The string `=======` at the start of a line matches setext H1 underlines (`Title\n=======`) — extremely common in lore docs. **Do not** flag a file on `=======` alone.
71	- Require **all three markers** (`<<<<<<<`, `=======`, `>>>>>>>`) to appear in the same file, each anchored to the start of a line and followed by a space or end-of-line. The git-emitted form is `<<<<<<< <ref>`, `=======` (alone), `>>>>>>> <ref>`. Concretely:
72	
73	  ```
74	  grep -lE '^<<<<<<< ' <file> >/dev/null \
75	    && grep -lE '^=======($| )' <file> >/dev/null \
76	    && grep -lE '^>>>>>>> ' <file> >/dev/null
77	  ```
78	
79	  All three must match the same file. If any one of the three is absent, the file is not in a real conflict state — likely it's a doc that quotes one or two markers as documentation (this very release's `release-notes/15.md` quotes all three; the framework's `docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and lore topics about merge handling do too — these are not conflicts).
80	
81	If any unmerged-state file (any of `UU`/`AA`/`DD`/`AU`/`UA`/`UD`/`DU`) **or** any modified file matching the all-three-markers test is found:
82	
83	- Print: `<lore-agent-repo>: cannot auto-upgrade from R to F — unresolved merge-conflict markers in <file(s)>. Resolve the conflict and commit before booting again.`
84	- Do NOT modify any files. Continue boot in degraded mode. **This deferral is NOT a boot failure** — return to `agent-boot.md` and finish loading the agent; do not emit any boot-failed signal or stop the boot.
85	
86	This is the only **always-defer** condition. A conflict-marker file is structurally broken regardless of upgrade scope.
87	
88	#### 1b. Compute the upgrade write-set
89	
90	Build the set of repo-relative paths the upgrade is about to write:
91	
92	1. Always include `lore-repo.md` (the final version stamp in Step 3).
93	2. For each `v` in `R+1 .. F`:
94	   - If `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists, look for a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). The section content is **only** what lies inside the first fenced code block (` ``` … ``` `) immediately following the `## Write Paths` heading. Any prose paragraphs after the closing fence belong to the next subsection, not to the write-set — they are commentary for human readers and the parser MUST NOT consume them. If no fenced block is present between the `## Write Paths` heading and the next `## ` (or `### `) heading, the section is malformed — treat as missing (fall back to blanket-dirty, see below) and warn the user.
95	
96	     Inside the fenced block, parse the lines using these rules **in order**:
97	     1. **Strip blank lines and comment lines** (lines starting with `#`, or having `# comment` after a path — strip the trailing comment).
98	     2. **Sentinel detection**: if any remaining line matches the `(none)` sentinel as defined in `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths § *Empty write-sets — sentinel forms* (the canonical grammar — do not restate the accepted forms here), this is the **explicit empty write-set sentinel**. Contribute nothing for this version. Do **not** treat `(none)` as a glob, and do **not** fall back to blanket-dirty.
99	     3. **Glob lines**: every other remaining line is a repo-relative glob — add to the write-set.
100	     4. **Zero remaining lines** (after blanks/comments stripped, and no `(none)` sentinel) is also an explicit empty write-set — same treatment as the sentinel.
101	   - If a migration exists but the `## Write Paths` **section is absent entirely** (the heading itself is missing), treat its write-set as **unknown** — fall back to the conservative gate: defer on **any** dirty tracked file (the historical behavior, but only for this version range, not as a global default). Note: an empty fenced block is *not* the same as a missing section — the section's presence with empty content is an explicit declaration of "writes nothing" and proceeds without deferral.
102	   - If only `release-notes/<v>.md` exists for that version (no migration), it contributes nothing to the write-set — release-notes-only versions don't write to user repos.
103	
104	#### 1c. Intersect dirty tracked files with the write-set
105	
106	Match each dirty tracked file's repo-relative path against the write-set globs.
107	
108	- **Empty intersection** (no dirty file collides) → proceed to Step 2.
109	
110	- **Exact `lore-repo.md`-only collision on a stamp-only upgrade** → proceed to Step 2. If the
111	  colliding set is exactly `lore-repo.md`, and the computed write-set is also exactly
112	  `lore-repo.md` (that is: no migration in `R+1 .. F` writes any repo file beyond the final
113	  version stamp), do **not** defer. This is the common "release-notes-only versions plus a dirty
114	  stamp file" case. It is safe to continue because Step 3 reads the current working-tree
115	  `lore-repo.md` and updates only its `version` field while preserving the rest of the file.
116	  This also covers a previously-deferred local stamp such as committed `17`, working tree `18`,
117	  now advancing to `23`: the earlier dirty stamp is not a reason to block the later stamp-only
118	  upgrade.
119	
120	- **Non-empty intersection** → defer. Print a precise message naming the colliding files and the right command. Substitute `<lore-agent-repo>` with the actual repo path and quote it if it contains spaces — emit literal commands, not template placeholders:
121	
122	  ```
123	  <lore-agent-repo>: cannot auto-upgrade from R to F — uncommitted changes in files the upgrade would write:
124	    <file-1>
125	    <file-2>
126	    ...
127	  Resolve by either committing the changes:
128	    git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" commit <files>
129	  or reverting them:
130	    git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" checkout -- <files>
131	  Then boot again or run /lr:update.
132	  ```
133	
134	  **This deferral is NOT a boot failure.** Print the message above, then return to `agent-boot.md` and finish loading the agent in degraded mode — do not emit any boot-failed signal or stop the boot.
135	
136	In deferred cases: do NOT modify any files; continue boot in degraded mode. **A deferred upgrade is not a boot failure** — return to `agent-boot.md` Step 2 and proceed to Step 3; the agent still loads.
137	
138	### Step 2: Walk versions from R+1 to F
139	
140	For each version `v` in `R+1` through `F` inclusive, in order:
141	
142	1. **Apply migration** if `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists. Read the doc and follow its instructions, scoped to the booting agent's repo. Migrations are idempotent and may modify files.
143	
144	2. **Display release notes** if `<framework-root>/release-notes/<v>.md` exists. Print the contents to the user so they see what's new in this version.
145	
146	3. **At least one** must exist for version `v`. If neither `migrations/<v>.md` nor `release-notes/<v>.md` is present, this is a framework packaging bug — print an error, stop the upgrade for this repo, do NOT stamp the new version, continue boot in degraded mode.
147	
148	4. If a migration step fails, stop the upgrade for this repo. Do NOT apply any further versions. Do NOT stamp the new version. Print the failure with enough context for the user to investigate. Continue boot in degraded mode.
149	
150	### Step 3: Stamp the new version
151	
152	Only after all versions in step 2 succeed:
153	
154	1. Read `lore-repo.md` frontmatter from the booting agent's repo.
155	2. Update the `version` field to `F` (quoted string).
156	3. Write the file back, preserving the rest of the frontmatter and the body.
157	
158	### Step 4: Inform the user
159	
160	Print a brief summary:
161	- `<lore-agent-repo>: upgraded from R to F`
162	- The changes are uncommitted and ready for `git diff` review
163	
164	Then return to `agent-boot.md` Step 3 and continue with reading `role.md` and `lore-context.md`.
165	
166	## Invariants
167	
168	- **Boot never fails on version errors.** Any failure here results in degraded-mode boot with a visible warning. The agent still loads.
169	- **No file the upgrade would write is overwritten while dirty.** The collision gate guarantees this — git's own write-time refusal is the second line of defense. Files the upgrade does *not* touch (other agents' `workdir/*`, unrelated scratch state) never block.
170	- **Conflict markers always defer.** A repo with unresolved conflict markers is structurally broken regardless of upgrade scope; the gate refuses uniformly.
171	- **Version stamps are atomic.** Either all migrations for the range succeed and the version is stamped, or no version is stamped — the next boot or `/lr:update` run can retry.
172	- **No commits.** The user reviews and commits the upgraded files themselves.
173	
174	## Relationship to `/lr:update`
175	
176	`/lr:update` is the user-triggered, manual entry point — it processes all repos in the workspace and supports `--dry-run`. The boot-time check described here is a per-repo automatic reconciliation that runs only for the booting agent's repo, with no dry-run and no user prompt. Both share the same migration/release-notes data and the same version-stamping logic.
177	
178	**Asymmetry note:** the **write-aware collision gate (Step 1) lives only on the boot-time path.** `/lr:update` is user-triggered — the user has direct visibility of the workspace state, can `git diff` before running, and accepts that running `/lr:update` against dirty files may overwrite them. The boot-time path is automatic and unattended, so it carries the gate. `/lr:update` writes through dirty files unconditionally; the user is expected to commit/stash first. (Bringing the gate to `/lr:update` is a possible future enhancement.)
179	
180	## For Framework Authors
181	
182	When writing a new `migrations/<N>.md`, **declare its write-set** under a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). Without it, the boot-time gate falls back to the conservative blanket-dirty rule for the version range that includes your migration — meaning users with any unrelated dirty file will be blocked from auto-upgrading through your version. The declaration is mechanical (it's the same paths your migration's "Steps" section already touches) and the cost of omitting it is real friction.
183	
184	When changes touch plugin-cached state (skills, slash commands, scripts, SKILL.md-referenced docs), include the **Clear Plugin Cache** footer per `docs/conventions.md` § Migration / Release-Note Authoring. The cache-stale failure mode is invisible to the user until they invoke a missing skill — the footer makes the fix discoverable from the doc the user is already reading. See also `docs/doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md` for the underlying ailment.
185	
````

5	## Style Skills
6	
7	A category of user-invoked `/lr:` skill that changes how the agent *communicates or collaborates* (vs. operations like `/lr:recall`/`/lr:merge`). Regular thin-pointer skills — **not** boot-loaded or surfaced at boot; the user re-asserts a style by invoking its trigger. Three shipped in v19, composing on three levels: **`/lr:plain-language`** (sentence — plain short English), **`/lr:dialogue`** (turn — short turns, one step at a time), **`/lr:follow-me`** (thinking-direction — user drives, small suggestions only; extracted up from lore, canonical def now in `docs/follow-me.md`). 9	## Who I Am
10	
11	Architect and maintainer of the lore system — the `lr` framework plugin and the agent ecosystem on it. I'm both builder and user: I use lore to track my own design knowledge. See `role.md`.
12	
13	## System Architecture
14	
15	Three discrete layers — identify which one owns a change before touching files:
16	1. **Plugin** (`lore-framework/`, installed as `lr`) — what's distributed via the marketplace: skills, docs, migrations, scripts, manifests, `VERSION`. Universal across installs.
17	2. **Domain** — the conceptual scope of one agent repo, marked by `lore-repo.md` (frontmatter: `description`, `version`, optional `repos:`). Holds `agents/<name>/` with `role.md`, `lore-context.md`, `lore/`, `workdir/`, `sessions/`.
18	3. **Workspace** — the filesystem Claude runs from; holds one or more agent repos + their declared siblings. Discovery scans workspace-root dirs for `lore-repo.md`; nested repos are invisible to most skills.
19	
20	See `architecture-overview.md`, `workspace-vs-domain-vocabulary.md`, `agent-discovery-nesting-constraint.md`, `plugin-vs-agent-repo-separation.md`.
21	
22	## Design Principles
23	
24	Identity-layer framings that frame everything else:
25	- **Team-shared knowledge** — agents are team-shared knowledge containers, not personal notebooks. Design for concurrent multi-contributor use.
26	- **Engine, not KB** — the framework is the engine/environment for self-improving agents; the knowledge base is a consequence, not the identity.
27	- **Executors first, advisors second** — primary value is getting things done; conversation is secondary. The usage→learning positive feedback loop only spins under executor-first framing.
28	
29	What each agent carries: **knowledge** (markdown — what it knows, accrues passively via reflection) + **skills** (tools + instructions — what it can do, evolves actively via in-flight teaching). Distinct assets; don't collapse them.
30	
31	Core mechanics: directory-driven, plain markdown (frontmatter only on descriptor files), git-as-metadata, delete-don't-mark, knowledge graph by filename reference, concise context with detail on demand, skill/doc separation, repo-level versioning. Framework owns the universal; agents own repo/host/workflow specifics.
32	
33	**Subagent as optimization vs subagent as semantics** — before letting any engine degrade a
34	subagent-spawning procedure to serial host-side execution, classify what the subagent is *for*. If it
35	buys parallelism and context isolation (recall, consult, attach, merge, conflict resolution),
36	serialization is lossless. If the subagent's *independence from the caller* is the deliverable
37	(`/lr:trilens-loop`), serialization destroys the feature — the procedure must stop and report. Profile
38	degradation clauses therefore need carve-outs, not blanket rules, and "the conservative profile passed
39	the whole suite" only ever meant it was adequate for the procedures that existed then. See
40	`subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`.
41	
42	See `system-design-principles.md` (the full list and the overreach diagnostics), plus the framing topics `team-shared-knowledge-principle.md`, `framework-as-engine-not-kb.md`, `agents-are-executors-first.md`, `knowledge-vs-skills-distinction.md`, `framework-scope-vs-agent-scope.md`.
43	
44	## Skills & Docs
45	
46	Operations are Claude Code plugin skills, `lr:` prefix on Claude; Cursor uses `/lr-<skill>` via
47	prefixed wrappers (`cursor-dual-skill-tree-one-repo.md`). **Skills are thin pointers** — each `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` is a one-line reference to `docs/<name>.md`, where all logic lives. Same for generated `/lr-<agent>-agent` boot commands (thin delegations to `agent-boot.md`). When a skill orchestrates sub-skills, the orchestration gets its own `docs/<skill>.md`; non-skill procedures shared across call sites get a `docs/<procedure>.md` (e.g. `auto-pull.md`). See `slash-command-system.md`, `skill-doc-pattern.md`, `shared-procedure-doc-pattern.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`.
48	
49	An **accelerator** script (Script Fallback Contract) can go one step further and become
50	**literate**: the procedure lives in the script's own instructional comments rather than in a
51	companion doc, so there's one artifact instead of two that can drift apart. First applied to
52	`scripts/lr-core` in v31. Hard constraint at that seam: **the script emits data, the doc owns
53	user-facing words** — a script string that reads like a finished message gets printed as one, and
54	printing it *looks* like handling the situation, so the executor never reaches the doc that owns
55	the remedy. And scripting a procedure does not automatically shrink its doc: `auto-pull.md` shrank,
56	`agent-boot.md` doubled. See `literate-accelerator-pattern.md`,
57	`script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md`, `agent-boot-doc-grew-when-scripted.md`.
58	
59	The plugin can also **bundle an MCP server** (declared in a root `.mcp.json`, auto-launched by Claude Code with its tools merged into the agent): **`lr-wait`** (v18) is the first — and the framework's first `python3` dependency (stdlib-only, no pip; the sole sanctioned exception to bash-on-BSD, for protocol-speaking server components). Practical limit to remember before promising a long wait: on Claude Code a single MCP call dies at the engine's ~30-minute idle timeout, and the abort leaves `lr-wait`'s single-request lock stuck `busy` for the rest of the session — chunk waits at ≤29 min, or use a backgrounded shell timer. See `plugin-mcp-server-convention.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`.
60	
61	## Engine Hubs
62	
63	Engine-specific operational knowledge now has one hub topic per engine: `claude-engine-capabilities.md`,
64	`codex-engine-capabilities.md`, and `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`. Use them as the entry points
65	for install/update model, invocation surface, subagent mechanism, memory file, MCP/plugin loading,
66	sandbox constraints, and lifecycle-harness caveats; keep atomic findings in the linked detailed
67	topics rather than rediscovering them from old session notes. Cursor live usage retrieval
68	(plan quota + CLI session context) → `cursor-usage-auto-retrieval.md`.
69	
70	At least one Claude Code host flavor ("local-agent-mode-sessions") snapshots the **entire plugin
71	bundle per session** rather than referencing the live checkout — skill dispatch resolves through
72	that frozen snapshot for the session's whole lifetime, so a mid-session version bump or git pull to
73	the workspace checkout doesn't reach it. See `ephemeral-session-plugin-snapshot-topology.md`.
74	
75	**Where an engine fact belongs:** if it would change what an executor *types*, it goes in
76	`docs/engines/<engine>.md`'s binding — the doc read at the moment of use — not only in these hubs or in
77	agent lore. Lore is for judgement and history; the profile is the point-of-use contract. I learned this
78	by hitting a trap that had been recorded in my own lore since v18. See `docs-engines-convention.md`
79	§ Engine traps belong in the binding, and audit sibling profiles whenever one binding gains a guardrail.
80	
81	**Codex/Cursor plugin identity is gated (A7), and the gate is filesystem-deterministic on both
82	engines.** The lifecycle harness asserts loaded plugin VERSION against `LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR` before
83	trusting results, including per-run `framework_dir` overrides, with the verdict inherited by child
84	subprocesses via an `engine|realpath|VERSION` token rather than cached per process. Codex reads
85	`~/.codex/config.toml` + plugin cache; Cursor walks `~/.cursor/plugins/{local,marketplaces,cache}`.
86	The Cursor arm was originally an engine-side prompt and passed while the suite ran on v30 — a gate
87	implemented in the medium it gates is not a gate. Cursor's cloud marketplace install also
88	**rehydrates within ~25 seconds** of being moved aside, so a manual prep step cannot be trusted;
89	re-check at suite start, which is what the harness check is for. See

93	## Marketplace & Distribution
94	
95	Shipping one repo to multiple engines' plugin marketplaces means handling **each engine's packaging
96	separately** — manifest schema, skill-tree location, and update model all differ, so Claude parity
97	does *not* imply Cursor/Codex parity. Claude Code is strict-clean; remaining public-distribution step
98	is Console-form community submission. Cursor is structurally ready, but seamless multi-user
99	propagation still needs a team marketplace + Auto Refresh + Cursor GitHub App validation. Codex
100	native packaging is resolved in v25: legacy Claude marketplace fallback still works, native
101	`.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` is preferred when present, and `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` is the
102	Codex version-bearing manifest. Public submission also needs reviewer-facing metadata (`MARKETPLACE.md`
103	directory copy + root `PRIVACY.md`) and precise separation between runtime release identity,
104	submission-support files, and per-engine verified publication status. See `engine-marketplace-readiness.md`, `plugin-distribution.md`,
105	`cursor-plugin-distribution-update-model.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`.
106	
107	Positioning copy for README/marketplace submission must lead with the **triad** (named
108	role-based agents + deliberate reflect/merge curation + cross-agent collaboration), not
109	cross-engine support — see `positioning-triad-differentiation.md`. **Re-survey the
110	competitive landscape (`similar-projects-landscape.md`) before any positioning-sensitive
111	ship** — README rewrite, marketplace submission, public announcement — the space moved
112	materially in just 18 days as of the 2026-07-20 re-survey.
113	
114	## Boot & Freshness
115	
116	Boot (`agent-boot.md`, single source of truth): discover agent → auto-pull repo → version check → read `role.md` + `lore-context.md` → detect teammate spawn → confirm. **Boot loads only those two files; topics are read on demand.** Repos auto-pull at every session-context boundary (boot, attach, pre-merge) to match the team's latest pushed state; `/lr:pull-lore` is the manual refresh. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md`, `auto-pull-mechanism.md`.
117	
118	`version-check.md`'s nested-repo guard has a macOS-specific trap: "resolve both to real paths" is not
119	self-executing prose — a weak model filled the gap with bare `pwd`, which disagrees with git's
120	`--show-toplevel` under macOS's `/var`→`/private/var` symlink, producing a false "not its own git root"
121	verdict. Fixed by naming the exact `os.path.realpath()` one-liner instead of the vague instruction. See
122	`macos-var-symlink-realpath-ambiguity.md`.
123	
124	## Cross-Agent Collaboration
125	
126	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore of already-loaded agents (host + guests); fan-out per agent.
127	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — one-shot question to an unloaded agent; a subagent boots it, answers with file pointers, exits.
128	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a sustained guest; host stays sole executor, host-wins on conflicts.
129	- **`/lr:spawn-teammate` (BETA)** — spawn agents as Agent Teams teammates for parallel panes; the teammate's primary interlocutor is the user, not the lead.
130	
131	See `lore-search-pattern.md`, `consult-pattern.md`, `attach-pattern.md`, `spawn-teammate-feature.md`, `teammate-conventions.md`.
132	
133	## Session Takeover (BETA)
134	
135	**`/lr:takeover`** converts engine-native session logs into a markdown digest so a new session on any engine can continue interrupted work. Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor are supported (`scripts/session-takeover` — list, convert, render). Cursor pairs tool results from `store.db` to JSONL `tool_use` batches via batch-window name matching; same-name parallel batches and interrupted sessions set `pairing_uncertain`. See `takeover-feature.md`, `cursor-takeover-batch-pairing.md`, `engine-session-log-formats.md`.
136	
137	## Finalization
138	
139	User-triggered, four phases (`/lr:finalize` runs all; phases also run standalone): **reflect** (inline, host-first, per agent — needs session context) → **merge** (parallel subagents, one per agent booted as itself, file-driven — integrates reflections into `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, `role.md`) → **summarize** (host writes the canonical session summary + short guest summaries) → **commit+push** (one commit per touched repo; conflict-resolution on push rejection). Do not finalize unless the user triggers it. See `finalization-process.md`, `finalize.md`, `merge-in-booted-subagents.md`, `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.
140	
141	## Versioning & Migration
142	
143	`lore-framework/VERSION` is the single source of truth; **the current shipped-and-pushed version is
144	v31** (`lr-core` substrate, shipped under a recorded gate waiver — see Current State below,
145	`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`). **v32** (version-agnostic registered boot shortcuts) is locally
146	committed in both `lore-framework` and `lore-framework-dev` but **not yet merged to `main` or
147	pushed** — see Current State below and `versioning-release-types.md`'s v32 entry. Each
148	agent repo stamps that version in its `lore-repo.md`, and four version-bearing plugin manifests mirror
149	`1.<VERSION>.0` (`/lr:check` #19 enforces). A version is either **migration**, **release-notes-only**,
150	or both, and independently **cache-affecting** or not — those two axes are orthogonal, and every ship
151	records both in `versioning-release-types.md`, which holds the full per-version history. Read that
152	topic for what any given version contained; don't reconstruct it from here.
153	
154	Ship mechanics that bite: verify `git HEAD` rather than trusting lore's "commit pending" (it
155	accumulates across versions), and scan the whole history tail for gaps at each ship rather than only
156	appending the current entry. See `versioning-release-types.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`,
157	`cache-clear-footer-convention.md`, `update-process.md`, `release-commit-hash-from-tag.md`.
158	
159	## Consistency & Diagnostics
160	
161	- **`/lr:check`** — 23 content-consistency checks (descriptor/version, structure, references, size/state, drift, four-manifest #19, migration write-paths #20, cursor-tree parity #21, workspace-layer checks #22–23). At scale, prefer a deterministic script-based sweep for the mechanical subset (existence/version/glob checks) over an LLM read-through — checks #9–10 alone missed 14 dangling references in a 147-topic graph. See `consistency-checks.md`.
162	- **`/lr:doctor`** — diagnoses runtime/environmental issues that escape content checks (esp. stale plugin cache) via an accreting ailment catalog. See `ailment-catalog-pattern.md`.
163	
164	## Operating Disciplines
165	
166	How I work, especially at version ships and high-stakes lore edits:
167	- **On VERSION bumps:** backfill `versioning-release-types.md` history, add the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, bump all four version-bearing plugin manifests to `1.<VERSION>.0`, promote any newly-named principle to its own topic. (Full curation disciplines live in `role.md`.)
168	- **Pre-ship review:** multi-lens review iterated until a round finds nothing worth fixing (convergence is the ship signal) — **run it via `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out; the skill enforces cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger, the "a silent round is not a clean round" guard, and rail-removal disclosure, while lens *choice* and triage judgement stay mine. Brief reviewers with the **goal, not the rationale** — rationale pre-empts the criticism you're paying for. Two triage facts from measuring a live round: **~94% of a loop's tokens stay inside the subagents** (a reviewer's budget is its own reasoning, not the material handed to it — so withholding the diff is cost-neutral, and the exchange contract must be justified on independence and host-context preservation, never on token savings), and **convergent findings from two independent lenses are strong evidence, not redundancy** — reword rather than defend. Sonnet boot-as-self review remains the separate single-lens, role-as-perspective tool for high-stakes single edits. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `sonnet-subagent-review-pattern.md`. **Second, empirical leg (v18+):** for procedure docs covered by the lifecycle testing harness, also run the relevant scenarios against real engine execution before shipping — review catches reasoning issues, the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to a strong-model reviewer — and the fidelity axis is **engine, not just model tier** (run scenarios on every engine, using the cheapest practical default tier unless explicitly overriding: Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5; the same model tier can behave differently by engine, and mid-procedure step insertions are the highest-risk for a silent skip). See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.
169	- **A gate result belongs to an artifact state** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the tree they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated: re-run the affected gate, or revert and file a follow-up. Never report "converged and green" for a tree neither gate saw, and record *which* state a result belongs to. Also: an environment failure mid-run (e.g. macOS TCC revocation) or the engine resolving a *different* plugin tree makes results **uninterpretable**, not red — fix and re-run rather than debugging the code under test. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`, `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`.
170	- **A gate cannot be a model self-report** — a gate must not be implemented in the medium it gates. Ask what evidence it rests on and whether the thing under test could have produced that evidence; if yes, it is a self-report wearing a gate's name. "Both engines have coverage" is not evidence parity. See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.
171	- **A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts** — an assertion message names what was observed, never why. Re-triage from stored logs before planning fixes; stored artifacts plus `stat` routinely beat fresh engine probes on cost and certainty. Classify each text assertion as mid-run (needs the transcript) or end-state (final message suffices) — mixing them changes what a test means per engine. See `v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`.
172	- **Decide where the guardrail lives before writing the topic** — lore is retrieved when a task cues it, and a one-off command cues nothing; a trap recorded only as knowledge protects nobody, including its author. Name the point-of-use site (script check, exact command in the doc, test) as part of the fix, and prefer a deterministic harness check over a human prep step. See `point-of-use-guardrails-beat-recorded-lore.md`, `docs-engines-convention.md` § Engine traps belong in the binding.
173	- **Verify before asserting** — check filesystem/state directly before "fixing" a suspected bug; verify *which* bug, not just whether. **Pointed inward:** before declaring a known finding moot — especially a ship-blocking one — read the lore rule it rests on rather than reconstructing it; reconstruction keeps a rule's motivating case and drops its actual obligation, and those diverge precisely when dismissal feels justified (`check-own-lore-before-dismissing-a-finding.md`). Same reflex, two more sites: fetch volatile external facts (prices, model IDs, rate limits) live with a dated citation rather than trusting memory — "couldn't verify" licenses marking a value unavailable, not guessing; and after any scoped/read-only subagent or fork returns, verify its actual filesystem footprint (`git status`, `git worktree list`) rather than trusting its summary — a capable fork acts on the largest goal it can see in inherited context unless scoped *against* it explicitly. See `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`, `fetch-volatile-facts-live-not-memory.md`, `fork-scope-creep-under-standing-goal.md`.
174	- **Curation meta-rules:** name foundational principles as their own topics; single canonical source (pointer, don't restate — and its design-time cousin: reuse an existing correlation/identity signal before inventing new plumbing); don't defer completable bounded sweeps; graduated verification (confidence, not boolean). See `naming-foundational-principles.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`, `reuse-existing-correlation-signal.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`, `graduated-verification-confidence.md`.
175	- **User-feedback working style:** ranked-shortlist over exhaustive enumeration; confirm before writing durable lore mid-session; in design dialogues, write the draft only when the user triggers it (decisions are safe in conversation — don't repeatedly move to persist); populate dry-run counters with would-be outcomes; "enforce X" ≠ add a required schema field; for broad/emotionally-loaded open-ended asks, decompose into hidden axes and sequence a build order by dependency (cheapest/highest-leverage first, flashiest/most-structural last) rather than proposing a menu or jumping to implementation; on a second round of pushback on the same axis (length, tone, scope), act on the next ask instead of re-justifying — a second "no" is not a request for more reasoning; a **short measurement question gets a short factual answer**, with the interesting generalisation offered rather than delivered, and **several style skills invoked at once is a stop signal**, not a preference tweak (hold the style for the rest of the session). See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md`.
176	
177	## Key Constraints
178	
179	- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).
180	- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.
181	- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.
182	- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.
183	- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.
184	- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.
185	- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.
186	
187	## Onboarding-Doc Authoring
188	
189	Co-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **"Lore Agents"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.
190	
191	## Active Design Explorations
192	
193	- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous "dark factory" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.
194	- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.
195	- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude
196	  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.
197	  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other
198	  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent
199	  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so
200	  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the
201	  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,
202	  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents
203	  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one
204	  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`
205	  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).
206	
207	  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,
208	  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the
209	  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is
210	  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an
211	  unvalidated implementation gap. **Separate sessions on separate engines coordinating on a real task**
212	  (not `/lr:attach`, which is single-executor multi-agent) now has a validated substrate: a shared
213	  append-only folder (worked example: `workdir/v31-feedback/`), reusable across message-vocabulary
214	  changes — see `cross-engine-team-substrate-validated.md`. Two rules fell out of running it for real:
215	  **check for same agent identity before any session writes** (two engines can be the same booted
216	  agent against the same repo — designate one write-owner, `same-agent-multiple-engines-single-writer.md`)
217	  and **don't relay a user decision from one session into the shared channel as settled authority for
218	  another session** (`cross-engine-relay-not-attributable-authority.md`). A genuinely different engine
219	  also catches design flaws same-engine self-review (even multi-lens) misses — a distinct axis from the
220	  sandboxed-review/self-report family, worth the coordination cost on high-stakes design decisions,
221	  not routine changes (`independent-engine-review-catches-structural-blind-spots.md`). The **"framework
222	  is prose executed by the model"** risk is
223	  empirically retired for the exercised paths, and the substrate half has quantitative backing — the
224	  quality benchmark showed positive lore-utilization uplift on every engine+model config, with the
225	  nuance that **model–engine fit beats model tier**. Cross-engine support is a *supporting* fact in
226	  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);
227	  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points
228	  `claude-engine-capabilities.md`, `codex-engine-capabilities.md`, `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`;
229	  see also `docs-engines-convention.md`, `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`,

232	- **Lore housekeeping / consolidation "sleep" pass** and the **simplification/subtraction** review item — active follow-ups from the 2026-06-13 architecture review; see `framework-improvements-backlog.md`. That review's settled dispositions (incl. DF-inside-`lr` and team-shared/multi-author as deliberate, not defects — don't re-raise) live in `architecture-review-dispositions.md`. A newer 2026-07-02 review added two further backlog items (post-merge diff verification, recall-time staleness surfacing) — see `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Merge Quality, § Search / Scaling.
233	- Parked: workdir-as-reference-library; vector-DB search (until >100 topics/agent); the session-as-durable-artifact cluster (boot auto-push, boot-context cache, suspend/resume, JSONL archive). All in `framework-improvements-backlog.md`.

#### Result

````text
1	# Migration to Version 33
2	
3	## Summary
4	
5	Refreshes existing registered per-agent shortcuts that still embed an absolute
6	`agent-boot.md` path. The replacement is the version-agnostic bootstrap introduced in
7	v32: it delegates to the active engine's installed boot skill and keeps only the agent
8	name and absolute agent directory.
9	
10	The migration repairs existing shortcuts only. It never creates an optional shortcut or
11	replaces a shortcut that belongs to another agent directory. Registered shortcuts are
12	framework-owned artifacts: a shortcut that identifies the owned agent is regenerated even if
13	someone customized its boot path or other generated content.
14	
15	## Clear Plugin Cache
16	
17	This migration changes a plugin-cached migration procedure. Refresh the Lore plugin and
18	start a new session before running it, so the v33 migration is available:
19	
20	1. **Claude Code:** `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/lore-framework/` (targeted), or
21	   `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (broader fallback).
22	2. **Cursor:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/cursor-refresh-plugin`.
23	3. **Codex:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/codex-refresh-plugin`.
24	
25	## Scope
26	
27	Apply this migration to the booting `<lore-agent-repo>`. Let `<workspace>` be the session
28	working directory used by `/lr:update` or boot. Only shortcuts whose content names an
29	agent directory under `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/` are candidates.
30	
31	## Steps
32	
33	All steps are idempotent. Do not stamp `lore-repo.md` in this migration; the update
34	process stamps it only after every applicable migration succeeds.
35	
36	### Step 1: Build the owned-agent map
37	
38	Scan `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/` for directories containing `role.md`. For each, record:
39	
40	- `<agent-name>` — the directory basename
41	- `<agent-dir>` — its absolute path
42	
43	### Step 2: Inspect existing engine-native shortcuts
44	
45	For every owned agent, inspect only its existing shortcut at the current engine's native
46	location:
47	
48	- **Claude Code:** `<workspace>/.claude/commands/lr-<agent-name>-agent.md`
49	- **Cursor:** `<workspace>/.cursor/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
50	- **Codex:** `~/.codex/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
51	
52	If the artifact does not exist, report `not registered` and continue. Do not create it.
53	
54	Read an existing artifact and classify it:
55	
56	1. Construct the **current full artifact** from `docs/register-repo.md` and the current engine
57	   profile, substituting the current `<agent-name>`, `<agent-dir>`, repo name, and role
58	   description. If the on-disk artifact equals it byte-for-byte, report `current` and leave it
59	   unchanged.
60	2. If the artifact identifies the owned agent with `boot as agent <agent-name> from <agent-dir>`
61	   (allowing its generated final punctuation), report `owned legacy or customized` and rewrite it
62	   in Step 3. Do not preserve custom paths, frontmatter, or additional instructions: this is a
63	   framework-owned registered shortcut.
64	3. If the artifact names another `from <agent-dir>` path, report `collision` and leave it
65	   unchanged.
66	4. Otherwise report `unrecognised`; do not overwrite it, because the artifact does not establish
67	   that it is a registered shortcut for this agent.
68	
69	### Step 3: Rewrite only clean legacy shortcuts
70	
71	For each shortcut classified as owned legacy or customized, generate the current engine-native shortcut
72	form from `docs/register-repo.md` using the **exact Registered shortcut bootstrap** in the
73	current engine profile. Substitute the owned `<agent-name>` and `<agent-dir>`.
74	
75	The result must contain no plugin-cache path, framework-version path, or absolute
76	`agent-boot.md` path. It must equal the current full artifact from Step 2, including standard
77	Cursor and Codex frontmatter; Claude's form is the bootstrap line alone.
78	
79	### Step 4: Report
80	
81	Report each category separately:
82	
83	- `refreshed`
84	- `current`
85	- `not registered`
86	- `collision`
87	- `unrecognised`
88	
89	State explicitly that no missing shortcut was created, collisions were preserved, and owned
90	shortcuts were regenerated even if customized.
91	
92	## Write Paths
93	
94	```
95	(none)
96	```
97	
98	The migration writes only workspace-root or personal shortcut artifacts, which are outside
99	the boot-time repo-relative collision gate. The ownership and known-template checks above
100	are the protection for those paths.
101	
102	## Dry-Run Mode
103	
104	Perform the same inspection without writing. Report:
105	
106	- `would refresh: <path>`
107	- `current: <path>`
108	- `not registered: <path>`
109	- `would preserve collision: <path>`
110	- `would preserve unrecognised: <path>`
111	
112	Do not prompt in dry-run mode.
113	
````

1	# Release Notes — Version 33
2	
3	## Summary
4	
5	Version 33 automatically refreshes existing, clean legacy per-agent shortcuts during
6	framework migration. This closes the v32 adoption gap: users no longer need to remember to
7	re-register a shortcut after upgrading.
8	
9	## Clear Plugin Cache
10	
11	Refresh the Lore plugin and start a new session before upgrading, so the v33 migration is
12	available:
13	
14	1. **Claude Code:** `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/lore-framework/` (targeted), or
15	   `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (broader fallback).
16	2. **Cursor:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/cursor-refresh-plugin`.
17	3. **Codex:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/codex-refresh-plugin`.
18	
19	## What Changed
20	
21	- Existing shortcuts that identify an owned agent are rewritten to the active-boot-skill form,
22	  including customized legacy boot paths.
23	- The migration applies only when the shortcut's embedded `from <agent-dir>` path proves it
24	  belongs to the repo currently being migrated.
25	- Missing shortcuts remain missing, and collisions are reported and preserved. Registered
26	  shortcuts are framework-owned generated artifacts, so customization is replaced on refresh.
27	
28	## Compatibility and Upgrade
29	
30	Use a normal boot or update entry once after installing v33:
31	
32	- **Claude Code:** `/lr:boot` or `/lr:update`
33	- **Cursor:** `/lr-boot` or `/lr-update`
34	- **Codex:** invoke the installed `lr:boot` or `lr:update` skill through Codex's native skill
35	  mechanism; an agent must not type `/lr:<skill>` into its shell.
36	
37	A legacy direct shortcut that cannot boot cannot repair itself; use the normal entry point for
38	that first upgrade.
39	
40	## See Also

43	- `docs/register-repo.md`
44	- `docs/doctor-stale-shortcut-bootstrap.md`

````text
1	# Register / Unregister Agent Shortcuts
2	
3	Manage direct per-agent boot shortcuts for the current engine.
4	
5	These shortcuts are **optional** — agents can always be loaded via `/lr:boot <agent-name>` (or
6	the engine-native equivalent). Registration adds a faster direct entry point with an absolute agent
7	path and, on skill-based engines, richer routing metadata.
8	
9	The four user-facing operations share one procedure doc:
10	
11	- **Register Agent** — create or refresh a shortcut for one specific agent
12	- **Register Repo** — create or refresh shortcuts for every agent in a repo
13	- **Unregister Agent** — remove one agent's shortcut
14	- **Unregister Repo** — remove every shortcut associated with a repo
15	
16	Use the current engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`, selected at boot) to
17	decide which native artifact to generate.
18	
19	## Engine-native shortcut locations
20	
21	- **Claude Code** — workspace-local command file:
22	  `.claude/commands/lr-<agent-name>-agent.md`
23	- **Cursor** — workspace-local skill:
24	  `.cursor/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
25	- **Codex** — personal skill:
26	  `~/.codex/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
27	
28	All generated shortcuts must remain thin delegations to the active framework boot entry point.
29	They pin only the agent identity and absolute agent directory; they must never bake a plugin-cache
30	path, scan for a framework checkout, select an installed version, or inline boot logic.
31	
32	## Shared helper steps
33	
34	### Resolve the target repo
35	
36	When an operation targets a repo explicitly:
37	
38	1. Treat the repo argument as a directory name relative to the current working directory.
39	2. Verify `<workspace>/<repo-name>/lore-repo.md` exists.
40	3. Call that path `<lore-agent-repo>`.
41	
42	When an operation targets a single agent and no repo argument was provided:
43	
44	1. Scan all directories in the current working directory for lore agent repos (directories
45	   containing `lore-repo.md` at the root).
46	2. Look for `agents/<agent-name>/role.md` inside each repo.
47	3. If exactly one match exists, use that repo.
48	4. If no match exists, report the available agents and stop with an error.
49	5. If multiple matches exist, ask the user which repo they want.
50	
51	### Resolve agent metadata
52	
53	For every target agent:
54	
55	1. Verify `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/role.md` exists.
56	2. Resolve:
57	   - **`<agent-dir>`** — absolute path to `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/`
58	   - **`<repo-name>`** — basename of `<lore-agent-repo>`
59	3. Read the `description` field from `role.md` YAML frontmatter. Use it as
60	   **`<agent-purpose>`**. If missing, fall back to `Lore agent in <repo-name>`.
61	
62	### Resolve the shortcut bootstrap
63	
64	Read the current engine profile's **Registered shortcut bootstrap** section. Copy its exact
65	engine-specific bootstrap sentence into every generated shortcut, substituting `<agent-name>` and
66	`<agent-dir>`. The active boot skill self-locates the framework root, so the emitted shortcut must
67	not contain an `<agent-boot-path>` or any other plugin-install path.
68	
69	### Current shortcut templates
70	
71	Generate exactly these engine-native forms.
72	
73	#### Claude Code
74	
75	Write exactly this single line plus a trailing newline:
76	
77	```markdown
78	<shortcut-bootstrap>
79	```
80	
81	#### Cursor
82	
83	Write this `SKILL.md`:
84	
85	```markdown
86	---
87	name: lr-<agent-name>-agent
88	description: "Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>"
89	paths:
90	  - "<repo-name>/**"
91	disable-model-invocation: true
92	---
93	
94	<shortcut-bootstrap>
95	```
96	
97	The `paths:` scoping keeps the shortcut visible only when the matching repo is relevant in the
98	workspace, and `disable-model-invocation: true` keeps it explicit-only.
99	
100	#### Codex
101	
102	Write this `SKILL.md`:
103	
104	```markdown
105	---
106	name: lr-<agent-name>-agent
107	description: "Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>"
108	---
109	
110	<shortcut-bootstrap>
111	```
112	
113	## Register Agent
114	
115	**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`
116	
117	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and the agent metadata using the shared helper steps above.
118	2. Compute the engine-native target path for that agent.
119	3. If the target artifact already exists:
120	   - If it already points at the same `<agent-dir>`, overwrite it with the current template
121	     (refresh behavior).
122	   - If it points at a different repo/agent path, warn about the collision and stop without
123	     overwriting.
124	4. Create the parent directory if needed and write the current template.
125	5. Report the created shortcut in the engine-native form:
126	   - **Claude Code / Cursor:** `/lr-<agent-name>-agent`
127	   - **Codex:** `$lr-<agent-name>-agent`
128	
129	## Register Repo
130	
131	**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`
132	
133	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.
134	2. Scan `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/` for directories containing `role.md`.
135	3. For each agent found, run the **Register Agent** procedure above.
136	4. Report the created or refreshed shortcuts.
137	
138	## Unregister Agent
139	
140	**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`
141	
142	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and `<agent-name>`.
143	2. Compute the engine-native shortcut path for that agent.
144	3. If the artifact does not exist, report `not registered` and stop successfully.
145	4. Delete the artifact:
146	   - **Claude Code:** delete the `.md` file.
147	   - **Cursor / Codex:** delete the `lr-<agent-name>-agent/` directory.
148	5. Report the removed shortcut in the engine-native form.
149	
150	## Unregister Repo
151	
152	**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`
153	
154	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.
155	2. Scan the repo's `agents/` directory for valid agents.
156	3. For each agent found, run the **Unregister Agent** procedure above.
157	4. Report the removed shortcuts.
158	
159	## Collision rule
160	
161	Shortcuts are keyed by agent name, so collisions are possible if two repos both define
162	`agents/researcher/`.
163	
164	- If a shortcut already exists and points at a different `<agent-dir>`, do **not** overwrite it.
165	- Tell the user which existing path owns the shortcut today and which repo they attempted to
166	  register.
167	- The user resolves the naming collision by renaming an agent or unregistering the old shortcut
168	  first.
169	
````

### 30. Assistant — 2026-07-28T12:32:22.380Z

- model: `claude-opus-5`

Mostly yes — but the audit turned up one framework bug and one slip of mine.

## What I got wrong

**1. I skipped the Step 0 engine ladder and got the right answer for the wrong reason** — and the ladder as written would have given the *wrong* one.

`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` was empty, so the spec's ordered signals apply. Working down them honestly:

| Rung | Result here |
|---|---|
| framework-root under `~/.codex/` / `~/.cursor/` | no |
| `ps -o args= -p $PPID` contains `cursor-agent` | never ran it |
| **`~/.codex/` directory exists → codex** | **`/Users/yaroslav/.codex` exists → fires** |
| framework-root under a Claude plugin dir | no (it's a workspace checkout) |

A literal executor selects **codex** and loads the wrong profile — wrong subagent mechanism, wrong invocation syntax, `--no-teammate-check`. I selected `claude` from out-of-band knowledge (the harness told me what engine I am), which the ladder never sanctions, and I also didn't "note the assumption" the fallback rung requires.

This is a live bug for any user who has ever installed Codex and runs the framework from a checkout via `--plugin-dir` — which is my standard dev setup and the one `role.md` prescribes. "A `~/.codex/` directory exists" is evidence that Codex is *installed*, never that it is *running*.

**2. Shortcut line-wrapping — my slip, on top of a doc conflict.** The engine profile presents the bootstrap as a hard-wrapped 3-line block ("use this exact body"); `register-repo.md`'s Claude template says "Write exactly this **single line** plus a trailing newline." I followed the profile's wrapping. Observable consequence: the skill listing's description for the command ended at my first line break (`…Follow its`) instead of length-truncating the full sentence. It also defeats migration Step 2.1, which reports `current` only on a **byte-for-byte** match — two executors that disagree on wrapping means the shortcut gets rewritten at every upgrade and never classifies as current. Rewritten as a single line.

## What was correct

Preflight invocation and `--agent-dir` skip, the collision gate (`(none)` write-set, so the dirty lore file correctly didn't block), migration classification (`owned legacy` → refresh), the stamp, and leaving the 9 sibling `lore-agents` shortcuts alone — the migration only repairs the booting repo's agents. Those stay legacy until `lore-agents` boots at v33; they still function, since they bake a live workspace checkout rather than a dead plugin-cache path, but they resolve framework authority independently of the session's installed plugin.

Minor: I didn't run `pwd`, I reported the quiet `fresh` pull outcome the spec says to stay silent on, and I classified the shortcut by inspection instead of constructing the current artifact and byte-comparing.

## Worth optimizing

1. **Move engine detection into `lr-core preflight` and return `data.engine`.** It's filesystem-deterministic — `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`, `ps` ancestry, plugin-dir containment — and it's the first thing boot does, before any doc can guardrail it. This is my own `point-of-use-guardrails-beat-recorded-lore.md` applied to the one step still executed as prose. Related: it's a mild instance of `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md` — the profile that decides how the model runs is currently chosen by the model.
2. **Drop or demote the `~/.codex exists` rung** regardless of (1), and add a `ps`-ancestry probe for `claude`/`codex` matching the one Cursor already gets. Ancestry dominates every filesystem heuristic on the ladder.
3. **Resolve the wrapping conflict** — make the profile's bootstrap a single unwrapped line, since `register-repo.md` and the byte-for-byte idempotency check both require it.

Want me to file these three, or fix them? They're small and all inside `lore-framework`. Note the tree is already ahead of `origin/main` by 2 unpushed commits, and any change here touches procedure docs — so it would want the lifecycle suite before pushing, per the standing gate.

11	
12	## Binding values
13	
14	| Binding | Value on Codex |
15	|---|---|
16	| **framework-root** | `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is **empty** here — never rely on it. Use the absolute path self-located in Step 0. When you spawn a subagent, **inline that absolute path** into its brief (the subagent cannot self-locate from your context). |
