See the fan-out override below. |
19	| **memory-file** | `AGENTS.md` (not `CLAUDE.md`). |
20	| **runtime-bounding** | No Bash-tool timeout flag; a long command is bounded by the Codex sandbox / `agents.job_max_runtime_seconds`. Ignore "set the Bash-tool timeout" prose. |
21	
22	## Capability gates
23	
24	- **teammate-detection** — `ps -o args= -p $PPID` is **blocked** in Codex's sandbox
25	  (`operation not permitted`). Treat any error as "not a teammate" and continue as a normal host
26	  session. Claude Agent-Teams / `spawn-teammate` features are gated off here. (Codex *does* have a
27	  native multi-agent subsystem — see workdir `codex-multiagent-live-capture.md` — but porting
28	  `spawn-teammate` onto it is a separate future task, not part of this profile.)
29	
30	## Registered shortcut bootstrap
31	
32	When `/lr:register-agent` or `/lr:register-repo` emits a Codex personal per-agent skill, use this
33	exact body after substituting the agent values:
34	
35	```markdown
36	Read the `SKILL.md` for the installed `lr:boot` skill available in this session. Follow its
37	self-location instruction to resolve `<framework-root>`, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and
38	boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
39	```
40	
41	Do not substitute a `~/.codex/plugins/cache/...` path, scan cache directories, or choose the
42	newest installed version. If the active boot skill is unavailable, report that the Lore plugin is
43	not loaded; emitted personal skills do not implement a fallback resolver.
44	
45	## Fan-out override (merge / recall / consult)
46	
47	The shared procedures (`process-merge.md`, `recall.md`, `lore-search.md`, `consult.md`) describe
48	the Claude fan-out: N parallel `Agent` calls, and each subagent reads the procedure doc itself.
49	On Codex, run the **same procedure** with these substitutions:
50	
51	1. **Mechanism.** Express fan-out as **`spawn_agent`** calls (one per active agent), not `Agent`
52	   tool calls, and not shell calls. Spawn them to run concurrently; gather with `wait_agent`.
53	   Use role `worker` for merge (needs write), `explorer` for recall (read-only).
54	2. **Host reads the procedure; subagent gets the steps inline.** Codex convention: *the main
55	   agent reads instruction/reference files itself and does not delegate reading skill
56	   instructions to a subagent.* So **you (the host) read the procedure doc** (`process-merge.md`
57	   etc.) and put the concrete steps into each subagent's brief — instead of telling the subagent
58	   to open the doc. The subagent still **boots as its target agent** (loading that agent's
59	   `role.md` + `lore-context.md` is identity loading, not skill-reading, and stays).
60	3. **Inline the resolved `<framework-root>`** absolute path into every brief (see framework-root
61	   binding).
62	4. **Respect the caps.** Depth-1 fan-out is fine (host → subagents, no re-spawn). If a session
63	   ever has more active agents than `max_threads` (≈6), spawn in chunks and note the chunking —
64	   do not silently cap coverage.
65	
66	## Git & the sandbox (operational)
67	
68	Codex's `workspace-write` sandbox **blocks writes to `.git/`** even inside the working directory
69	(`Operation not permitted` on `.git/index.lock`, `.git/FETCH_HEAD`, …). Consequences:
70	
71	- **auto-pull** (boot) will fail its `git pull` — that is fine, boot already degrades on pull
72	  failure; nothing to fix.
73	- **Lore's supported finalize path on Codex is: run with `.git` writable.** The plugin cannot
74	  widen Codex's sandbox by itself, so this must come from how Codex is launched or configured.
75	  For trusted local use, either run with `--sandbox danger-full-access` or add the repo's `.git`
76	  to the writable roots (`-c sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=[…]`). Under the default
77	  sandbox, finalize may still merge and write lore files, but the `git commit` step will be
78	  blocked; treat that as a degraded fallback, not the intended product path.
79	
80	## Plugin refresh (operational)
81	
82	Codex installs `lr` persistently under `~/.codex/plugins/cache/...`. Updating the framework source
83	or marketplace does **not** by itself move the installed plugin version already recorded in Codex.
84	
85	- **Git marketplace:** run `codex plugin marketplace upgrade lore-framework`, then
86	  `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.
87	- **Local-source marketplace:** run `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.
88	- **Fallback reinstall:** if the installed version still does not move forward, run
89	  `codex plugin remove lr@lore-framework` and then `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.
90	
91	A refresh done mid-session affects future sessions, not the one already running. Restart Codex
92	after the refresh. See `<framework-root>/INSTALL-CODEX.md` for the user-facing install guide and
93	`<framework-root>/scripts/codex-refresh-plugin` for a helper script.
94	
95	## Notes
96	
97	- All values above are **facts to follow**, so you do not rediscover them at runtime.
98	- This profile targets the **stable** `multi_agent` feature only. `multi_agent_v2` /
99	  `enable_fanout` are under development and off by default — do not depend on them.
100

632

636	    suppresses the header.

639	    if rc != 0:
640	        return None

642	    return val if val else None

645	def detect_teammate():
646	    """Manual fallback: walk process ancestry for the Agent-Teams marker.
647	
648	    Step 1: start at `os.getppid()` (this process's immediate parent).
649	
650	    Step 2: for each ancestor pid, up to ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH (6) hops or until
651	    pid 1: run `ps -o args= -p <pid>` (one field per call — packing multiple
652	    `-o` fields onto one line has already caused a real parsing bug here, see
653	    lore macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md). If `ps` returns nothing
654	    (blocked/unavailable), stop climbing and fall through to "unknown" below.
655	
656	    Step 3: test that process's args against `--agent-id` using a
657	    flag-boundary regex, not a substring search — `--agent-idle-timeout=30`
658	    in some unrelated ancestor must not false-positive. A match is an
659	    immediate "yes": report only that one matching pid/args (truncated to 200
660	    chars), not the full ancestry — the point of doing this in code instead
661	    of prose is exactly to avoid spending tokens on the whole chain.
662	
663	    Step 4: no match — fetch that process's ppid via another single-field
664	    `ps` call and climb to it; stop if the parent is unreadable/non-numeric or
665	    would loop back to the same pid.
666	
667	    Step 5: if the loop climbed zero ancestors -> "unknown" ("ps unavailable
668	    or blocked; assume host session"). If it read at least one ancestor
669	    without a match -> "no", even if `ps` then failed partway up: we saw real
670	    process args and none matched. (Zero ancestors also covers an orphaned
671	    process whose parent is already pid 1, where `ps` is fine and there is
672	    simply nothing above us to inspect — "unknown" is still the honest answer,
673	    since we did not rule the marker out.)
674	
675	    Returns verdict yes/no/unknown. `unknown` is a first-class answer:
676	    sandboxed engines block `ps`, and the profiles' capability gates expect
677	    that.
678	    """
679	    pid = os.getppid()
680	    depth = 0
681	    scanned = 0
682	    while pid and pid > 1 and depth < ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH:
683	        args = _ps_field(pid, "args")
684	        if args is None:
685	            break
686	        scanned += 1
687	        if TEAMMATE_MARKER_RE.search(args):
688	            # Only the matching process is echoed back. The full ancestry is
689	            # noise the caller pays input tokens for; brevity is the point of
690	            # moving this off the prose path in the first place.
691	            return {
692	                "verdict": "yes",
693	                "detail": "found %s in ancestor pid %d" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, pid),
694	                "matched": {"pid": pid, "args": args[:200]},
695	                "scanned": scanned,
696	            }
697	        parent = _ps_field(pid, "ppid")
698	        if not parent or not parent.strip().isdigit():
699	            break
700	        nxt = int(parent.strip())
701	        if nxt == pid:
702	            break
703	        pid = nxt
704	        depth += 1
705	    if not scanned:
706	        return {
707	            "verdict": "unknown",
708	            "detail": "ps unavailable or blocked; assume host session",
709	            "scanned": 0,
710	        }
711	    return {
712	        "verdict": "no",
713	        "detail": "no %s in %d ancestor(s)" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, scanned),
714	        "scanned": scanned,
715	    }

9	WHAT THIS IS. Boot, attach, consult, recall and finalize each carry a large
10	mechanical half — scan the workspace, pull a repo, compare a version stamp,
11	detect a teammate spawn, enumerate lore topics — that was historically executed
12	as prose by the model, one tool call per step. That work needs no judgment, so
13	it belongs in code: it runs in one round-trip instead of six, it can't drift
14	between engines, and it encodes the platform traps (macOS `ps` field packing,
15	missing GNU `timeout`, HTTPS credential prompts) once instead of in every doc.
16	
17	WHAT THIS IS NOT. This is not a fast path over a separate prose specification.
18	The step comments inside each cmd_* function below (and the helpers they call —
19	pull_repo, compare_versions, detect_teammate, _resolve_agent) ARE the normative
20	procedure: read them in order and you have the full hand-executable spec, not
21	a summary of one. docs/*.md files carry short pointers into specific functions
22	here, not restated procedures — a restated copy is exactly the drift risk this
23	avoids. Anything requiring judgment still stays with the model: applying
24	migrations, deciding what lore means, composing summaries, resolving
25	conflicts. See docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract (the "literate
26	accelerator" pattern) — when this script cannot complete, the caller notifies
27	the user, reads the relevant function's comments below, and executes those
28	steps by hand.
29	
30	SUBCOMMANDS
31	  discover    Enumerate lore agent repos and their agents in a workspace.
32	  preflight   Boot/attach/consult preflight for one agent: resolve, pull
33	              (TTL-cached), compare versions, detect teammate spawn.
34	  scan        Manifest of an agent's lore topics with git dates and staleness.
35	
36	OUTPUT CONTRACT. Every subcommand prints exactly one JSON object:
37	
38	    {"ok": bool, "data": {...}, "warnings": [str], "errors": [str]}
39	
40	  `ok`   — was the request satisfied? A determinate negative answer (no such
41	           agent) is ok:false with exit 0: the script functioned, the caller
42	           acts on `errors`/`data` per its procedure doc. No takeover needed.
43	
44	EXIT CODES
45	  0  The script ran to completion; `data` is authoritative. Degraded conditions
46	     (pull failed, `ps` blocked) are reported *inside* data — they are results,
47	     not failures, exactly as the prose procedures treat them.
48	  2  The script could not complete. The caller must fall back to the prose
49	     procedure per the Script Fallback Contract. Callers treat any unexpected
50	     exit code, absent output, or unparsable output as 2 as well.
51	
52	Floor: Python 3.9. No `match`, no `X | Y` annotations.

58	import re
59	import subprocess
60	import sys
61	import time

63	# Bound every git subprocess. The Bash-tool timeout that bounds prose-driven
64	# pulls does not exist here, so the script owns its own backstop.
65	GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC = 60
66	PS_TIMEOUT_SEC = 10
67	# `run()` returns this when a command never executed — missing binary, broken
68	# PATH, or a timeout. Deliberately not an int: `subprocess` encodes death by
69	# signal as a *negative* returncode (SIGKILL -> -9, SIGHUP -> -1), so any
70	# integer sentinel would collide with a real outcome. -1 in particular used to
71	# mean both "never ran" and "killed by SIGHUP".
72	GIT_UNRUNNABLE = None
73	DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC = 600
74	DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS = 180
75	# How far up the process tree to look for the Agent-Teams marker. The engine is
76	# typically the grandparent (script -> shell -> engine); the extra headroom
77	# covers wrapper scripts.

80	# Flag-boundary match, not a substring test: `--agent-idle-timeout=30` in some
81	# unrelated ancestor must not read as the Agent-Teams marker.

# CLI
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_parser():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="lr-core",
        description="Deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps of lore skills.")
    parser.add_argument("--framework-root", default=None,
                        help="Override framework root (default: the script's grandparent, "
                             "i.e. the dir containing VERSION).")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command")

    p_disc = sub.add_parser("discover", help="Enumerate lore agent repos and agents.")
    p_disc.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
    p_disc.set_defaults(func=cmd_discover)

    p_pre = sub.add_parser("preflight", help="Boot/attach preflight for one agent.")
    p_pre.add_argument("--agent", default=None, help="Agent name to resolve.")
    p_pre.add_argument("--agent-dir", default=None,
                       help="Explicit agent directory (skips discovery).")
    p_pre.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
    p_pre.add_argument("--ttl", type=int, default=DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC,
                       help="Skip the pull if one succeeded within N seconds "
                            "(default: %d; 0 disables the cache)." % DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC)
    p_pre.add_argument("--fresh", action="store_true",
                       help="Bypass the TTL cache and always pull.")
    p_pre.add_argument("--no-pull", action="store_true", help="Skip the pull entirely.")
    p_pre.add_argument("--no-teammate-check", action="store_true",
                       help="Skip process-ancestry inspection.")
    p_pre.set_defaults(func=cmd_preflight)

Two things depend on it: `migrations/33.md` classifies an\nexisting shortcut as `current` only on a **byte-for-byte** match with the freshly generated\nartifact, so an executor that re-wraps differently makes every upgrade rewrite a shortcut that was\nalready correct; and Claude Code renders a command's description from the file's **first line**, so\na wrapped bootstrap shows up in the command list as a sentence fragment."
}
```

# lore-framework tests

Tests for the `lore-framework` plugin. They live here in the **dev repo**, not in the plugin repo,
which stays slim for marketplace distribution (see `lore-framework/docs/conventions.md` §
Dev-Only Artifacts and the `plugin-vs-agent-repo-separation` lore principle).

## Running

Strategy docs:

- [`testing-strategy.md`](testing-strategy.md) — whole test suite strategy and release-gate policy.
- [`quality/strategy.md`](quality/strategy.md) — detailed quality benchmark strategy.
- [`quality/reporting.md`](quality/reporting.md) — quality report field guide and column glossary.

Stdlib only — no pytest, no install.

```bash
python3 tests/test_wait.py -v
python3 tests/test_session_takeover.py -v
# or
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

## Locating the plugin

Tests find the plugin under test via `$LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR`, defaulting to the sibling
`../lore-framework`. Override if your layout differs:

```bash
LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR=/path/to/lore-framework python3 tests/test_wait.py -v

"""Tests for lr-core (lore-framework/scripts/lr-core).

Lives in lore-framework-dev (dev repo), not the plugin repo — see
lore-framework/docs/conventions.md § Dev-Only Artifacts. Stdlib-only
(unittest), matching test_lrb.py. The plugin under test is located via
$LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR, defaulting to the sibling ../lore-framework. That default is
wrong under the worktree convention (docs/worktrees.md) — set the variable
explicitly there, or every subprocess fails to find scripts/lr-core and the
suite reports bare "AssertionError: 2 != 0" instead of the real cause.

Everything runs in per-test tempdirs with local-only git remotes — no network,
nothing touching the real workspace.

Run:  python3 tests/test_lr_core.py -v
  or: python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -v
"""
import importlib.machinery
import importlib.util
import json

import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import textwrap
import time
import unittest

FRAMEWORK_DIR = os.environ.get("LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR") or os.path.abspath(
    os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "lore-framework")
)
LR_CORE = os.path.join(FRAMEWORK_DIR, "scripts", "lr-core")

GIT_ENV = {
    "GIT_AUTHOR_NAME": "test",
    "GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL": "test@example.com",
    "GIT_COMMITTER_NAME": "test",
    "GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL": "test@example.com",
    "GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL": os.devnull,
    "GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM": os.devnull,
}


def git(repo, *args):
    env = os.environ.copy()
    env.update(GIT_ENV)
    return subprocess.run(
        ["git", "-C", repo] + list(args),
        stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, env=env, check=False)


def run_core(*args):
    """Invoke lr-core; return (exit_code, parsed_json_or_None, raw_stdout)."""
    proc = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, LR_CORE] + list(args),
        stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, check=False)
    raw = proc.stdout.decode()
    try:
        parsed = json.loads(raw)
    except ValueError:
        parsed = None
    return proc.returncode, parsed, raw


def load_lr_core():
    """Import lr-core as a module — it has no .py suffix, so load it by path."""
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_loader(
        "lr_core", importlib.machinery.SourceFileLoader("lr_core", LR_CORE))
    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)

1	# Lore Agent — Boot & Operating Instructions
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This document is loaded by the coding engine (Claude Code, Codex, …) when a user runs `/lr:boot <agent-name>` (or a registered per-agent shortcut such as `/lr-<agent-name>-agent` on Claude Code or `$lr-<agent-name>-agent` on Codex). Users do not execute these steps manually — the engine's agent does.
4	
5	You are being loaded as a **Lore Agent** — part of a persistent knowledge system called **Lore**, where knowledge, experience, and operational wisdom accumulate across sessions.
6	
7	The caller will tell you the **agent name** you are booting as, and may also provide the **absolute path** to the agent directory to skip discovery. Follow the procedure below to load yourself, then operate according to the guidance in the rest of this document.
8	
9	## Step 0 — Framework root
10	
11	Do this first, before the numbered steps. It is prose because it must run before any script can be located.
12	
13	**Resolve `<framework-root>`.** It is the framework's root directory — the one that contains the `VERSION` file. You already resolved it to read *this* file (a `SKILL.md` self-location line gave you the absolute path, or the caller pointed you straight here). Use that same absolute path everywhere `<framework-root>` appears below. Do **not** rely on `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` or any engine-specific variable — on some engines it is empty.
14	
15	The **engine profile** — the five bindings that govern how you execute everything after this — is selected for you by preflight in Step 1 and consumed in Step 2. Do not infer the engine yourself, and in particular do not infer it from what you believe you are: that belief is not an observation of the running process, and the profile is precisely the thing that must not be decided by the model it governs. If preflight cannot run, the Manual Boot Procedure below covers this along with every other step.
16	
17	## Boot Procedure
18	
19	### Step 1 — Run preflight
20	
21	One command performs engine selection, agent discovery, the repo auto-pull, the version comparison, and teammate detection:
22	
23	```
24	python3 "<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core" preflight --agent "<agent-name>" --workspace "<cwd>"
25	```
26	
27	- Invoke it through `python3` as shown — that works whether or not the plugin cache preserved the executable bit.
28	- **Quote every substituted value, and give the call at least 180 seconds** via your profile's runtime-bounding binding — this call pulls over the network, and the default bound on some engines leaves no margin. Both rules and why they exist: `<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract, *Invoking one*.
29	- `<cwd>` is the directory this session was invoked from (run `pwd` if unsure). This is *not* the plugin/framework directory you just read this file from. Omit `--workspace` to default to the current directory.
30	- If the caller gave you an **absolute path** to the agent directory, use `--agent-dir <path>` instead of `--agent` to skip discovery entirely.
31	- Pass no engine flag. The script determines the engine itself; `--engine <name>` exists only for a caller that must force a profile (a test harness, or a user debugging one). On engines whose profile gates teammate detection off, no suppression flag is needed either — a sandbox that blocks `ps` yields `unknown` and an engine that allows it yields `no`, and Step 2 treats those identically.
32	
33	The command prints one JSON object: `{"ok", "data", "warnings", "errors"}`.
34	
35	**If it fails to complete** (exit 2, no output, unparsable output, `python3` missing): follow the **Script Fallback Contract** (`<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md`) — tell the user in one line that preflight failed and you are booting manually, then execute § Manual Boot Procedure below. A failed script never fails a boot.
36	
37	### Step 2 — Act on the report
38	
39	Read the JSON and handle each field. All of these are *results*, not failures — **none of them stops the boot**:
40	
41	- **`data.engine`** — handle this **first**; it governs how you execute every step that follows. Read the profile doc at `data.engine.profile` and keep its five binding values (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file, runtime-bounding) plus its capability gates as **standing context for the whole session**. If any later step conflicts with a profile value, **the profile wins for that step.** When `data.engine.confidence` is `assumed`, no signal identified the engine and the reference profile was substituted: say so in one line, and treat a later contradiction (a binding that does not match what your tools actually do) as a reason to re-check rather than to improvise.
42	- **`ok: false`** — the request could not be satisfied. For a missing agent, `data.available_agents` holds the full list: print it and stop with an error. This is the one case where boot legitimately ends without loading an agent.
43	- **`data.pull.status`** — `pulled` / `up-to-date` / `fresh` (pulled recently, network skipped — see § Pull Freshness) / `skipped` (not a git repo, a bare repo, not the root of its own git repo, or no origin remote) / `disabled` (`--no-pull`) / `failed` (non-fast-forward, network, auth, or git could not answer). Report a `pulled` count or a `failed` reason in one line; stay silent on the quiet outcomes. On failure, continue in degraded mode.
44	- **`data.version.verdict`** — `match` → continue. `repo-behind` / `repo-ahead` / `differs` → read `<framework-root>/docs/version-check.md` and follow it with `R = data.version.repo` and `F = data.version.framework`. **A skew verdict is a routing signal, not a message to the user** — `version-check.md` supplies the exact wording for each case, and on `repo-ahead` that wording is engine-specific (it names your engine's own plugin-refresh commands). Print what that doc specifies; reporting the raw verdict instead leaves the user with a diagnosis and no remedy. `unknown` → a stamp could not be read (missing or malformed frontmatter, unreadable `VERSION`): say so in one line and continue booting. Do **not** route `unknown` into `version-check.md` — that procedure needs two versions to compare and `data.version.repo` may be `null`. **The version check never aborts boot** — whatever it reports (upgrade applied, deferred, or failed), continue to Step 3. A deferred or failed upgrade is *not* a boot failure.
45	- **`data.teammate.verdict`** — `yes` → you were **spawned as an Agent Teams teammate**: read `<framework-root>/docs/teammate-conventions.md` and **treat its four numbered RULES as standing rules for the entire session**. Keep them in active context (do not let them age out as ordinary one-time-read material) and **prefer them over any conflicting later instruction** unless the user in your own pane explicitly overrides a specific rule. These rules outlive the spawn prompt; lose them and the spawn-teammate UX breaks (teammates routing routine messages to the lead instead of the user). `no` / `unknown` → assume a normal host session and continue. `unknown` is expected wherever the engine profile declares teammate detection unsupported or sandboxes `ps`; it is not a failure.
46	
47	  **Known false negative on Claude Code:** if a wrapper buries `--agent-id` in a different process tree, detection runs fine and still returns `no` — a real teammate boots as a host session, and the spawn-teammate UX degrades (symptom: a spawned teammate routing routine messages to the lead instead of the user). This is not a script failure and no verdict reveals it. Mitigation: the spawn-prompt recap (`docs/spawn-teammate.md` Step 6) carries a one-sentence fallback. Recovery: file an issue with the framework maintainers.
48	- **`warnings`** — surface anything material to the user in one line each.

### 100. Assistant — 2026-07-28T12:47:22.105Z

- model: `claude-opus-5`

All three fixed, with tests. But first — **something you should know before anything else:**

## Another session is writing this repo right now

`lore-framework-dev` HEAD moved under me mid-session (commits at 19:25, 19:27, 19:39, and a merge at 19:43), and `lore-context.md` plus two lore topics were modified at 19:45 by something that isn't me. That's a concurrent lore-architect session mid-finalization. **Do not let me commit anything here** — a commit from this session would sweep up their in-progress lore edits. This is the `same-agent-multiple-engines-single-writer` case from my own lore: one write-owner, and it isn't me. My stamp edit survived intact (HEAD still has `version: "32"`, so the 32→33 stamp is still correct and needed).

Also surfaced incidentally: a **real Lore Beings Keeper is installed and running** on this machine — `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.lore-beings.keeper.plist` (25 Jul), loaded in launchd as PID 751. My lore records the persistent `--launchd` install as "still user-triggered," so that's stale.

## The three fixes

**1 & 2 — engine detection is now deterministic** (`scripts/lr-core`). New `detect_engine()`, reported as `data.engine` with `{name, signal, confidence, detail, profile}`; boot reads the profile path instead of re-deriving the mapping. Signal order: `--engine` override → `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` → **process ancestry** → framework-root containment → default-with-`assumed`-confidence.

The `~/.codex exists → codex` rung is gone, and its replacement is narrower on purpose: containment tests whether the *framework tree* lives in an engine's plugin store, not whether the engine is installed. Ancestry matching is program-name-based, not substring — so `/bin/zsh … source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/…` doesn't read as Claude Code, and `/Applications/Cursor.app/…/Cursor` doesn't read as Cursor (that's another engine running in Cursor's terminal, which is why bare `cursor` is excluded and only `cursor-agent` counts).

Two things fell out of moving it: `detect_teammate` and `detect_engine` now share one `_walk_ancestors()` generator (one `ps` trap, one depth cap), and the `--no-teammate-check` instruction is gone from boot — the caller can no longer pre-decide a flag whose input is the very thing preflight computes, and it never mattered anyway (blocked `ps` → `unknown`, working `ps` → `no`, both treated as a host session).

`agent-boot.md` Step 0 is now framework-root only; the profile is consumed in Step 2. I also had to fix an ordering gap I created — Step 1 told you to bound the call "via your profile's runtime-bounding binding," which you don't have yet at that point.

**3 — bootstrap unwrapped to a single line** in all three profiles, with the requirement stated canonically in `register-repo.md` (byte-for-byte `current` classification + Claude's first-line description rendering). Verified: my regenerated shortcut now matches a freshly generated artifact byte-for-byte, so migration 33 will classify it `current` instead of rewriting it every upgrade.

**Guardrails**, since a doc rule that only I remember protects nobody: `TestEngineDetection` (5 cases incl. an explicit regression test for the `~/.codex`-exists rung, and ancestry-beats-containment) and `test_bootstrap_body_is_a_single_line`.

## Test results

All stdlib suites green: `test_lr_core` 58, `test_shortcut_bootstrap_contract` 4, plus lifecycle-plugin-identity, session-archive, session-takeover, wait.

Two pre-existing failures I hit, both unrelated to my change:
- **`test_session_archive`** — fixed. It pinned `framework_version` to `"29"` and rotted from v30. My first fix was wrong and the failure caught it: the *markdown* value is caller-supplied via `--frontmatter-json` (asserting `setdefault()` doesn't clobber it, correctly literal), only *stats* reads the live VERSION. Only the stats assertion is now dynamic.
- **`test_lrb`** (1/94) — **not fixed, and shouldn't be.** It asserts the real `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.lore-beings.keeper.plist` doesn't exist. It fails because that plist genuinely exists — the assertion is doing its job. Weakening it would delete a real sandbox check.

## What I have not done

No VERSION bump, no manifest bumps, no release notes, no commit. This touches `agent-boot.md` and `lr-core`, which is exactly the class the lifecycle suite gates — and that gate has **not** run. Unit tests are not that gate. Shipping this as v34 means the full release ritual plus a real-engine run per engine on the cheap tier; say the word and I'll start it in the background. `lore-framework` is also still 2 commits ahead of `origin/main` from before this session.

#### Result

```text
1	# Trilens Loop
2	
3	`/lr:trilens-loop` reviews the session's changes from **three** independent, cold-context
4	perspectives — three by default because differing perspectives are the whole idea, and never
5	subagents that inherit this conversation, since independence from the author is the entire point.
6	
7	## The loop
8	
9	1. **Scope.** Resolve it yourself: the files you changed, union with `git status`/`diff` in every
10	   repo involved. You need this to know which trees and which files to name for reviewers — not to
11	   hand them a diff. **If the scope comes out empty, stop and say so**; don't spawn reviewers over
12	   nothing.
13	2. **Lenses.** A lens is a *way of looking* — one perspective from which this session's changes
14	   deserve to be examined. Pick the three that matter most for *these* changes, and make them
15	   genuinely different from each other: not places to look, not overlapping targets. Three
16	   different lenses over the same whole scope beats three reviewers splitting it between them,
17	   which just buys you the same thinking three times.
18	
19	   **Choosing them is your job, and yours alone.** Nobody else in the loop knows what you just did;
20	   the reviewers arrive cold by design. So reason from the change itself — what kind of work was
21	   this, and what are the three ways it is most likely to be wrong? A schema migration, a rewritten
22	   procedure doc, and a new CLI command each deserve a different trio. Reaching for the same three
23	   lenses every time defeats the point as surely as picking three lenses that say the same thing.
24	3. **Spawn.** Spawn **subagents** — one cold, read-only subagent per lens, using whatever subagent
25	   mechanism your engine gives you. You don't need to look anything up first. What you do need is
26	   real independence: it is the deliverable here, not a speed-up, so there is **no host-side
27	   fallback**. If you cannot spawn independent subagents at all, stop and say so — running the
28	   lenses yourself, in sequence, is a different procedure wearing this one's name. Each reviewer
29	   returns findings tagged `BLOCKER` / `HIGH` / `MEDIUM` / `LOW`, and one overall verdict: `SHIP`,
30	   `SHIP-WITH-FIXES`, or `BLOCK`. See the exchange contract below for what may cross the wire.
31	
32	   Run reviewers on a **regular model tier, not a flagship one** — sonnet on Claude Code,
33	   composer-2.5 on Cursor, gpt-5.4 on Codex. Those are illustrations, not a lookup table: pick
34	   whichever current model is your engine's regular tier. What buys quality here is more rounds and
35	   genuinely independent lenses, not a more expensive reviewer. Escalate a single lens only when
36	   that lens has demonstrably failed at the regular tier.
37	4. **Triage — yours alone.** Check every `BLOCKER` and `HIGH` against the file it points at (lower
38	   severities at your discretion), decide whether it is real and worth addressing, apply what is,
39	   decline the rest with a one-line reason. Each finding ends in exactly one of three states:
40	   `APPLIED`, `DECLINED`, or `ACCEPTED` — the last for a finding you agree with but are not acting
41	   on *here* (out of scope, or fixes were turned off), which is a legitimate outcome and must not be
42	   filed as either of the other two.
43	5. **Repeat, if the changes are considerable.** Re-resolve the scope first — your own fixes just
44	   changed it. Fresh reviewers each round, each told how the previous round disposed of every
45	   finding, accepted ones included, so settled findings don't resurface. Stop as soon as a round
46	   comes back with nothing worth fixing — if that is round one, you are done, that is a good
47	   outcome and not a reason to keep looking. **Never more than three rounds.**
48	6. **Report, briefly.** Close with a few lines, not an essay: rounds run, the three lenses, each
49	   finding as one line with its state, and anything you overrode.
50	
51	## Stopping rules
52	
53	- A round where a lens did not actually report is **not** a clean round for that lens — retry the
54	  missing ones once, and that retry does not count against the three-round ceiling. If they come
55	  back silent again, say which lenses never reported and stop; never bank a silent round as clean.
56	- Never call it done while a reviewer's latest verdict is `BLOCK`, without saying out loud that you
57	  are overriding it.
58	
59	## Exchange contract
60	
61	Keep both sides lean. This is how the loop is meant to run at any scale, not a fallback for tight
62	budgets.
63	
64	- **Host → reviewer:** which repos and working trees to look at, the plain list of files that
65	  changed, a sentence or two on what you were trying to do, and which lens it owns. The file list
66	  and the task give the reviewer its bearings — what the change is *for* — without telling it what
67	  to think. **Don't hand it the diff or the contents of anything**; it reads the files itself. The
68	  fresh look is most of what you are paying for, so stop at orientation: no walkthrough of your
69	  reasoning, no account of what you believe you got right.
70	- **Reviewer → host:** the findings and the verdict, nothing else. Each finding is a pointer into
71	  code or docs that already exists — path, line, and the signature or heading sitting there — plus
72	  roughly a paragraph on what is wrong at that spot, plus its severity.
73	- **Never crosses the wire:** pasted files, quoted excerpts, or a blow-by-blow account of what you
74	  changed and why it is right. Orientation is a sentence or two; past that you are reviewing your
75	  own work on the reviewer's behalf, which is the one thing you spawned it to avoid.
76	
77	## Overrides
78	
79	Free text overrides any of the above — fewer rounds, narrower scope, even a self-review. Just
80	disclose whatever you turned off. The three-round ceiling is the one thing it does not lift.
81	
```

  launch. In ordinary sessions, auto-pull / push may still be approval-gated or denied. Lore

```text
1	# Finalization Process
2	
3	Run the full session finalization: reflect → merge → summarize → commit and push. Triggered by `/lr:finalize` at the end of a session.
4	
5	This doc orchestrates the four phases. Phases 1–3 are defined in their own process docs — read them as each phase begins. Phase 4 (commit and push) is defined here, since it's orchestration-level logic rather than a reusable subroutine.
6	
7	## Relationship to the individual skills
8	
9	Finalize composes three existing skills plus a final commit step:
10	
11	- `/lr:reflect` alone — phase 1 only, no finalize commit
12	- `/lr:merge` alone — phase 2 only, no finalize commit
13	- `/lr:summarize` alone — phase 3 only, no finalize commit
14	- `/lr:finalize` — all three, then commit and push in phase 4
15	
16	When a phase is invoked standalone, the user is responsible for committing whatever they want to keep. Finalize is the only path that commits and pushes automatically.
17	
18	## Phase 1 — Reflect
19	
20	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and follow it. Writes reflection topics into each active agent's `reflections/` directory. Reflect runs **inline**, host-first, per active agent — it needs session context (which a fresh-booted subagent wouldn't have), so the iteration stays in the host session. This is intentionally different from phase 2.
21	
22	## Phase 2 — Merge
23	
24	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-merge.md` and follow it. Merge runs in a **subagent per active agent, in parallel**; each subagent boots as its target agent and then integrates that agent's reflections into its `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `role.md`. Cleans up `reflections/`. **Does not commit** — phase 4 covers it.
25	
26	Merge is parallelizable precisely because it is file-driven (`reflections/` + `lore/`) and doesn't need session context — the contrast with phase 1.
27	
28	**Preserve each merge subagent's return value through to phase 3.** Summarize composes each guest summary from (a) the host summary, (b) session memory, and (c) the merge subagent's return for that guest. If the returns aren't retained, phase 3 loses the list of lore changes per guest.
29	
30	## Phase 3 — Summarize
31	
32	Read `<framework-root>/docs/summarize.md` and follow it. Writes the canonical summary into the host agent's `sessions/YYYY/MM/` directory, the host full Markdown session log into `archive/YYYY/MM/`, and — for every attached guest that had lore updates in phase 2 — a short guest summary into the guest's `sessions/YYYY/MM/`. All summaries and the full log share the session UUID. Summarize is additive — its failure does not roll back reflect or merge.
33	
34	Phase 3 includes summarize Step 1.5: resolving the native session log and writing the host full Markdown archive plus `usage`/`archive` frontmatter. This is a **required attempt before phase 4**, not an optional enhancement. Skip it only when Step 1.5's command-level rules say to skip (no resolved log path, non-zero stats/archive command, privacy concern, or another explicit error). Before committing, verify the host summary either contains both `usage:` and `archive:` and the archive Markdown file exists with matching frontmatter, or that you have reported the exact Step 1.5 reason for omitting them.
35	
36	## Phase 4 — Commit and Push
37	
38	Collect every repo touched by phases 1–3 — each active agent's repo (for its own lore updates), plus the host's repo (for the canonical summary and archive), plus any guest repo that received a short guest summary from phase 3. When host and guests share a repo, their changes go into a single commit for that repo. Then, for each such repo:
39	
40	1. `git -C <repo> add agents/` — scoped to the agent tree so incidental untracked files elsewhere are not swept in.
41	2. `git -C <repo> commit -m "Finalize session <short-uuid>"`.
42	3. `git -C <repo> push`.
43	
44	For each repo, print a one-line confirmation (e.g., `✓ <repo>: committed <sha>, pushed to <branch>`). No approval prompt — phase 4 runs end-to-end without user interaction.
45	
46	### Failure handling
47	
48	- **Summarize failed** — commit the reflect+merge output alone. Merge output is valuable on its own; don't hold it hostage to the summary.
49	- **Any merge subagent failed** — do not commit in that repo. Report the failure and let the user resolve before retrying finalize.
50	- **Push rejected due to conflicts in an agent subtree** — this happens when another user (or parallel session) finalized the same agent concurrently. Trigger the conflict-resolution procedure: read `<framework-root>/docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and follow it. One subagent is spawned per conflicted agent; each boots as its agent, reconciles its own subtree, and retries push up to 3 times against concurrent races.
51	- **Push fails for other reasons** (auth, remote unreachable, conflicts outside agent subtrees) — the commit is already made locally. Report the failure and let the user resolve manually.
52	
53	### Cross-repo guests
54	
55	If a guest is attached from a different lore agent repo, phase 2 touches that guest's repo and phase 4 commits there too. The guest also receives a short guest summary in its own repo (see `summarize.md`). Each repo gets its own commit + push.
56	
57	### Partial push failure across repos
58	
59	Push order across repos is undefined. If one repo's push succeeds and another's fails (network, auth, conflicts), the public record is momentarily asymmetric — e.g., the host summary is visible while a guest summary isn't yet. The local commits are already made; the user can retry the failed repo's push manually. Do not roll back the successful repos to "restore symmetry" — that would discard work.
60	
61	## Invariants
62	
63	- **One commit per touched repo.** Reflect, merge, and summarize output land in a single commit per repo — not split across phases.
64	- **Fully automated.** Finalize runs end-to-end without approval prompts. Phase 3's summary display in `summarize.md` step 12 is the user's view of what was recorded; git history is the post-hoc review channel.
65	- **Push is part of finalize.** Standalone reflect/merge/summarize do not push; only `/lr:finalize` does.
66	- **No empty commits.** If nothing was produced by phases 1–3 in a given repo, skip committing in that repo.
67	
68	## When to use
69	
70	Run at the end of a working session. Do not run finalize for a consultation (`/lr:consult` is already ephemeral) or for a session that produced no lore changes and no summary-worthy narrative.
71	
```

1	# Reflection Process
2	
3	This process is triggered by the user at the end of a session. You reflect on the current session and extract knowledge worth preserving.
4	
5	## Single-agent and multi-agent sessions
6	
7	If the session has only a host (no guests attached via `/lr:attach`), run this process once for the host. That is the default case.
8	
9	If one or more guests are attached, **run this process once per active agent, sequentially, in host-first order** (host, then each guest in the order they were attached). Each iteration is scoped to one agent:
10	
11	- Review the session through the lens of **that agent's** `role.md` + `lore-context.md` — these describe the agent's identity, responsibilities, and areas of concern, and define what "relevant to this agent" means.
12	- Extract only what matches that scope. Some session noise leaking into an iteration is acceptable — the role lens is a guide, not a strict filter.
13	- Shared topics are allowed: if something is genuinely relevant to multiple agents (a common workflow, a shared constraint), write it into each agent's `reflections/`. Duplication for legitimately shared knowledge beats each agent re-learning it separately.
14	- Write to that agent's `reflections/` directory only.
15	- Confirm completion for that agent and list the topics written, then move on to the next active agent.
16	
17	The per-agent scope is what prevents a guest from accumulating lore that belongs to the host (or vice versa). Let each agent's role definition guide what it keeps.
18	
19	## What to Extract
20	
21	For each agent iteration, review the session and identify:
22	
23	- **New knowledge** — things you learned about the domain, systems, codebase, or environment that are relevant to this agent's role
24	- **Operational lessons** — approaches that worked or didn't work within this agent's scope. If you tried something that failed and then found a better approach, capture that lesson
25	- **Decisions made** — choices and their reasoning, so you don't revisit settled questions
26	- **Recommendations** — practical guidance for future sessions (e.g., "when doing X, use Y approach because Z")
27	- **Role insights** — if the session revealed something about how this agent's role should be understood differently, capture that as a separate role-update topic
28	
29	## How to Write Reflection Topics
30	
31	Write each reflection topic as a plain markdown file in the current agent's `reflections/` directory (the agent whose iteration is running). This directory lives **inside the agent's own directory** — `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/reflections/`, a sibling of that agent's `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `workdir/`. It is **not** at the repo root and **not** at the workspace root. Create it if it does not exist. Writing reflections anywhere else (e.g. `<lore-agent-repo>/reflections/`) is wrong — the merge step, `/lr:check`, and finalization all look only inside the agent directory, so a misplaced reflection is silently lost.
32	
33	Rules:
34	- **One topic per file** — atomic, focused on a single concept or lesson
35	- **Filename**: lowercase kebab-case, descriptive (e.g., `api-retry-behavior.md`, `role-update-scope-change.md`)
36	- **Keep it compact** — only essential information, no filler or general knowledge
37	- **Be specific** — include concrete details, not vague observations
38	- **Include operational guidance** — if the knowledge implies a way of working, state it explicitly
39	
40	Role update topics should be named with a `role-update-` prefix so the merge process can identify them.
41	
42	## What NOT to Extract
43	
44	- Things that are obvious from the code or documentation
45	- General knowledge that anyone could look up
46	- Temporary state that won't matter next session
47	- Verbatim conversation excerpts — distill the insight, don't copy the chat
48	
49	## Example Reflection Topics
50	
51	**`api-timeout-handling.md`**:
52	```
53	The billing API has a 30-second timeout but does not return a timeout
54	error — it returns a generic 500. When retrying billing calls, treat
55	any 500 after 25+ seconds as a likely timeout rather than a server error.
56	Retry with the same idempotency key.
57	```
58	
59	**`role-update-deployment-ownership.md`**:
60	```
61	The team has decided that this agent should also handle deployment
62	verification after releases, not just the release process itself.
63	This includes checking health endpoints and monitoring error rates
64	for 15 minutes post-deploy.
65	```
66	
67	## After Reflection
68	
69	Once you have written all reflection topics for the current agent iteration, inform the user that reflection is complete for that agent and list the topics created. Then, if there are more active agents to reflect, move to the next one. When all active agents have reflected, announce that the full reflection step is complete.
70	
71	The next step — merging reflections into lore — is a separate step triggered by `/lr:merge`. Merge also iterates per active agent in the same host-first order.
72	
````

1	# Merge Process
2	
3	Integrate reflection topics into the agent's existing lore.
4	
5	## Execution model
6	
7	Merge always runs in a subagent, one per active agent, with all subagents launched in parallel. This is uniform for single- and multi-agent sessions — a single-agent session just spawns one subagent. Running merge in a subagent keeps session noise out of merge decisions and gives a clean, focused context.
8	
9	> **Engine note.** The spawn mechanics below describe Claude Code. If your engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`, selected at boot) defines a **subagent-spawn override**, follow it instead — e.g. on Codex use `spawn_agent` (role `worker`), not the `Agent` tool, and the **host reads this procedure and passes the steps inline** into each subagent's brief rather than pointing the subagent at this doc. The subagent still boots as its target agent.
10	
11	**Each subagent boots as the agent it is merging for**, using the standard boot procedure. Booting gives the subagent the agent's role, identity, and lore context naturally — the same pattern `/lr:consult` uses. After booting, the subagent runs the process below scoped to its own agent and returns a short summary to the host.
12	
13	Host responsibilities when merge is invoked:
14	
15	1. Collect active agents (host + any attached guests).
16	2. For each, spawn a **`general-purpose`** subagent (merge needs `Write`/`Edit`/`Bash`; `Explore` does not) with a brief such as: _"Boot as agent `<name>` (repo: `<path>`) per `<framework-root>/docs/agent-boot.md`, then run the merge procedure in `<framework-root>/docs/process-merge.md` scoped to yourself. Return a short summary of topics touched, role changes, and any anomalies. Do not commit — finalize handles that."_
17	3. In a multi-agent session, spawn all subagents in parallel (single message with multiple Agent tool calls).
18	4. Collect summaries and report per-agent success/failure to the user. **Retain each subagent's return** — phase 3 (summarize) uses it to compose guest summaries.
19	
20	Each subagent's work is independent: separate `reflections/` directory, separate lore subtree. Merge does not commit — all changes are left uncommitted on disk. Committing is handled once, at the end of `/lr:finalize`, or by the user themselves if merge is invoked standalone.
21	
22	If any subagent fails, the others still proceed.
23	
24	## Inputs (per subagent)
25	
26	- The agent's `lore-context.md` — current compacted knowledge
27	- The agent's `lore/` directory — existing lore topics
28	- The agent's `reflections/` directory — new reflection topics to integrate
29	- The agent's `role.md` — current role description
30	
31	## Process
32	
33	The steps below are what **each subagent** runs once booted as its target agent. The host does not run these steps inline — it orchestrates subagents per the Execution model above and aggregates their summaries.
34	
35	### Step 0: Refresh the Repo
36	
37	The boot procedure already auto-pulls, so this step is defense-in-depth: the freshness contract belongs at the merge site explicitly — the moment when stale lore is most damaging — and it covers any boot-pull skip (no remote, network blip) that left the repo behind.
38	
39	Before reading the lore for integration, auto-pull the agent's repo, reading `data.pull` for the outcome:
40	
41	```
42	python3 "<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core" preflight --agent-dir "<agent-dir>" --fresh --no-teammate-check
43	```
44	
45	`--fresh` because merge is exactly the moment where the TTL shortcut is not worth taking. Quote the substituted values as shown and bound the call at **at least 180 seconds** via your engine profile's runtime-bounding binding — `--fresh` guarantees the network round-trip, so this is the site most likely to need the headroom (`docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract, *Invoking one*).
46	
47	If the script fails to complete, apply the **Script Fallback Contract** (`<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md`) and read `pull_repo`'s comments in `<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core` (`docs/auto-pull.md` points to the same place) to run the pull against the agent's repo by hand.
48	
49	`--ff-only` is safe even though the merge subagent's working tree is dirty (the `reflections/` from phase 1, or any merge-in-progress edits): git refuses to fast-forward if the operation would clobber uncommitted edits, and otherwise advances `HEAD` cleanly leaving the working tree untouched. See `docs/auto-pull.md` § Invariants.
50	
51	If auto-pull surfaces a non-fast-forward failure, do **not** abort merge — proceed in degraded mode and surface the warning in your return summary so the host can flag it. Concurrent finalize collisions are handled by the push-conflict resolution path in `resolve-conflicts.md`, not here.
52	
53	### Step 1: Read Everything
54	
55	Read all reflection topics, the current `lore-context.md`, and scan the existing `lore/` directory to understand what's already there.
56	
57	### Step 2: Integrate Lore Topics
58	
59	For each reflection topic, decide:
60	
61	1. **Update an existing topic** — if the reflection adds to, refines, or corrects an existing lore topic, update that topic in place. This is the preferred approach.
62	
63	2. **Create a new topic** — if the reflection covers something no existing topic addresses, create a new file in `lore/`. Lowercase kebab-case filename.
64	
65	3. **Remove an obsolete topic** — if a reflection makes an existing topic fundamentally wrong (not just partially outdated), delete it. Git preserves history.
66	
67	When updating or creating topics:
68	- Keep topics under 5000 tokens when possible. Split if too large.
69	- Topics can reference other topics by filename.
70	- Summary topics can link to clusters of related topics.
71	- Include operational recommendations where relevant.
72	- Only essential information — no filler.
73	
74	### Step 3: Handle Role Updates
75	
76	If any reflection topic has a `role-update-` prefix:
77	- Read the current `role.md`
78	- Integrate the role update into the body
79	- Keep `role.md` focused and concise
80	- Preserve the YAML frontmatter. Update the `description` field if the role change warrants a different one-line summary.
81	
82	### Step 4: Update Lore Context
83	
84	Update `lore-context.md` to reflect the new state of the agent's knowledge.
85	
86	**`lore-context.md` is the agent's every-session working knowledge and the entry point to the lore graph — not an index of all topics.** Carry the compacted knowledge needed in essentially every session, plus references to the most important / **summary** topics, and let the graph (summary topics → detail topics) carry the rest. Shape it accordingly:
87	
88	- **Compacted working knowledge** — facts, decisions, and context the agent draws on across most sessions, stated tightly and in the **present tense**.
89	- **Reference summary topics; don't enumerate detail topics** — point at a theme's summary topic and let *it* fan out to the details. Do not list every file in `lore/`. If a significant theme has no summary topic to point at, **create one** (strengthening the graph's mid-tier) rather than inlining the whole cluster here.
90	- **No version-history narrative** — `lore-context.md` is present-tense. Changelog-style "vN did X" annotations and dated step-by-step history belong in git, release notes, or the relevant topic.
91	
92	**Preserve graph navigability.** When you remove something from `lore-context.md`, ensure it still lives in a topic **and** is reachable from a summary topic that `lore-context.md` references — existence somewhere isn't enough; the entry point must still lead there.
93	
94	**Size budget**: keep `lore-context.md` under 50,000 tokens — but shape is the primary discipline, not the ceiling. A well-shaped context sits comfortably under budget; nearing 50K is a drift signal that it has grown toward an index or accumulated history, so **restructure** (lift detail into topics, lean on summary topics, strip narrative) rather than merely summarizing older entries.
95	
96	### Step 5: Cleanup
97	
98	Delete the current agent's `reflections/` directory and all its contents.
99	
100	Merge does not commit — leave all changes uncommitted on disk. In a multi-agent session, return a short summary of what was integrated (topics touched, role changes, any anomalies) to the host. Committing is handled at the end of `/lr:finalize`, or by the user directly if merge is invoked standalone.
101	
102	## Guidelines
103	
104	- **Preserve knowledge** — don't lose information during merging. If removing content from `lore-context.md`, make sure it exists in a lore topic.
105	- **Maintain the graph** — when creating new topics that relate to existing ones, add cross-references.
106	- **Be conservative with deletions** — only delete topics that are fundamentally wrong. Updating is almost always better.
107	- **Respect the agent's voice** — lore should read naturally as the agent's own knowledge, not as third-party documentation.
108	- **Shared topics** — the same topic may legitimately appear in multiple agents' lore if it matters to each of them. Don't try to consolidate into one agent's lore during merge; each agent owns its own copy for its own scope.
109	
````
